# Claims and evidence

- Batch 179 provenance/trace: packages/sciviz/tests/provenance.test.js proves runnable intermediate mutation/removal history, stable saved resource IDs and noncolliding future IDs, explicit recording limits, metadata hashes/versions, PNG image-chunk identity and CRC/cancellation bounds, safe namespaced SVG JSON, and full Unicode PDF information. packages/sciviz/tests/provenance-browser.mjs exercises PNG/SVG provenance and trace generation on all 24 existing scientific demos, MRI trace execution on both backends, and PDF provenance for MRI, NOAA wind and the surface scene. The record follows PROV-DM concepts but does not claim PROV-O graph conformance or cryptographic authorship. No speed result is claimed.


### Standalone DOF precision requirement

DOF refuses the LDR compatibility tier at setup and restore before allocating
replacement resources. The existing default scene is byte-identical between
full and half-float, while LDR exceeds the original image rule. Evidence:
dof/tests/lower-tiers.test.js and the package's registered test:gpu:tiers,
with unchanged parity assertions. No speed or complete lower-tier option
certification claim. [Scope and sources](../dof-tier-requirement.md).

### Native volume range repair

Parsed native glTF volume tint preserves optical depth until world-path scaling,
so mesh-space transmittance underflow no longer destroys recoverable light.
Proof: beer-distance.test.js, native-volume-path.test.js and
physical-map-renderer.test.js; test-native-volume.mjs retains twenty existing
exact pixels and adds eight underflow cases on both GPU backends. This is a
bounded slab/range repair, not full volume transport parity or a speed claim.
See [research and limits](../native-volume-range.md).

### Fixes K2 job 1: linear native display glass

Claim: native gltf-pro glass on display-encoded scene targets combines transmitted and lit light in linear values. The materials adapter snapshots each surface's background, decodes the two light contributions and encodes their sum. Proof: packages/materials/test/native-glass-browser.mjs fails before the fix and checks one/two overlapping surfaces at 1x/4x on both GPU backends against an independent transfer-function reference (one 8-bit quantization step). CPU ownership and shader regressions live in packages/materials/tests/native-glass.test.js. Scope: the native sightline-tint path, preserving its existing tone mapping; no equivalence claim for refracted HDR rendering or measured speed. Research sources and costs are in the operational playbook and limits.

### K2 React packed consumer

The packed React 18.3.1 consumer runs its component lifecycle assertions against installed package modules, including portal and decal StrictMode ownership. Tooling-script assertions use the explicit contributor repository and retain every original assertion. Evidence: modern:test:packages -- --packages=react, effects-extra.test.js, portals.test.js and the relocated tooling regression packed-react-repository.test.js. The consumer fixture declares Playwright types and supplies its own JavaScript/image test support; no source tree or new runtime dependency is shipped.

## Batch 187: lightmap visibility

Opaque back faces block area-light visibility and diffuse gathers. Front-side emission cannot pass through their
backs. Evidence: global-path.test.js tests both bounce modes and direct/indirect terms; global-browser.mjs compares
unblocked and blocked baked-light pixels on native and compatibility rendering, with no validation errors or retained
meshes required. The existing path-bounce, mesh, directional and progressive-bake features come from Batch 54.
The Cornell comparison is an improvement with a residual loss, not an accuracy-parity claim: the recorded baseline
MAE 0.069543 becomes 0.048730 with path mode, one bounce and 32 bounce rays (versus the baseline's quad mode and 16).
The maximum error falls from 0.900128 to 0.353979. All 881 reference texels are retained; no timing was measured.

Review 3 splats: progressive AbortSignal cancellation rejects pending async
iteration with exact reason identity, requests iterator cleanup once even when
it remains pending, and stops between buffered batches. Regression inputs and
event-order assertions are in packages/splats/test/progressive.test.js. Source
ownership limits and research comparisons are in docs/splats-progressive.md.
No new GPU algorithm or speed claim.

Review 3 (2026-11-10): attaching MToon preserves standard PBR/unlit primitives in
the same model. Evidence: packages/rigging/tests/mtoon.test.js captures visible
body/outline pipelines across opaque, mask and blend queues;
packages/rigging/tests/mtoon-light.browser.mjs compares the actual standard
foreground pixel before/after attachment on both backends. Sources and repro are
in docs/rigging-mtoon.md. No speed claim or new backend capability.

Batch 175 job 1: optional sciviz/omezarr streams selected regions from OME-NGFF 0.4/0.5, N5 and Neuroglancer precomputed multiscale volumes through the existing Zarr ROI reader and brick renderer. A 10^12-sample metadata-only fixture opens without chunk IO; its single-sample read requests one 8-byte chunk. Original CC BY 4.0 light-sheet IDR, OpenOrganelle N5 and sharded FlyEM bytes produce separate 32^3 regions and nine resident bricks (105,040 GPU bytes) on both backends. Every IDR/N5 ROI sample matches an independent numcodecs/Python reference. FlyEM's existing JPEG decoder differs from Pillow by at most one sample value. Evidence: packages/sciviz/tests/omezarr.test.js, scripts/audit-sciviz-cloud.py, packages/sciviz/tests/omezarr-browser.mjs. Codec/storage variant limits and nearest-neighbour LOD semantics are explicit in limits.md. No billion-voxel full-residency, timing, or universal codec-parity claim.

Batch 190 job 2: optional `oit/precise` removes half-float accumulation saturation while retaining the existing weighted approximation. At both 1x and 4x on native WebGPU and full WebGL2, eight half-opacity layers with radiance [1000,500,250] read [996,498,249] in the HDR scene target, against analytic [996.09375,498.046875,249.0234375]. The same scene in half-float OIT reads [16.3125,16.3125,16.3125]. Reordering gives the same result, ordinary radiance matches within 0.001, complete edge images match within one 8-bit count, and opaque occlusion plus a real PBR asset pass. This claims f32 accumulation range, not unlimited radiance, exact depth sorting or extra transmission support. No speed result.

Batch 190 job 1: optional `oit/peel` provides exact depth-ordered alpha transport at distinct per-pixel raster depths on native WebGPU and full WebGL2 compatibility. The four analytic layers differ by at most 0.66 of 255 on the tested GPU at scene MSAA 1x and 4x, and reversing registration changes no pixel. Crossing sheets swap order per pixel; a two-layer budget keeps the nearest two and opaque surfaces hide farther ones. Post and transparent canvas alpha checks pass. This is single-sample peeling, with the limits in limits.md, not a claim of sample-frequency edge reconstruction or refraction. Public rival behavior and research comparisons are in clean-room.md; no performance comparison is claimed.

Batch 256 job 4: ten tests in `packages/netcode/tests/migrate.test.js` establish
confirmed-state recovery through successive host departures under loss/jitter,
rollback of a partial commit, arbitrary-target handoff, stale-epoch rejection,
prepare interruption, detached state copies, cancellation, hard deadlines,
message forwarding, invalid packet/option rejection, a departing handoff target
and a survivor departing during the resume barrier. A synchronous-transport
regression fails before moving recovery-deadline initialization ahead of peer
notification; completed recovery must not arm a stale timer. This covers the requested
P2P host migration row in the documented trusted connected-mesh model; external
partition consensus and membership admission are outside this claim.

Batch 256 job 3: `packages/netcode/tests/generate-rust.test.js` compiles and runs
standalone generated Rust. One hundred full/delta pairs and 1,000 corrupted or
truncated full/delta inputs agree with both the runtime codec and TypeScript
generator. Native Rust values independently reproduce 25 full/delta encodings.
Explicit hostile scalar, length, mask, map id/overlap, quantization, recursive
schema and keyword-name checks also pass. This establishes the batch's same-byte
Rust generator row within its documented recursion limit; no speed claim.

Batch 256 job 2: `packages/netcode/tests/presence.test.js` covers two RoomServer
instances, detached/filterable listings, remote owner refusal after capacity is
reserved, lease expiry, atomic claims, namespace isolation, cancellation/timeouts,
handler errors, failed-heartbeat shutdown ordering and shared SQLite connections.
Two real child processes share a WAL database; one reserves the other's room and
a real WebSocket client joins that owner and receives its state. Closing an owner
withdraws its routes. This proves the batch's multi-process discovery/join row
against the documented Colyseus ownership model, not distributed simulation.

Batch 256 job 1: lobby tests (`packages/netcode/tests/lobby.test.js`) establish
metadata isolation/filtering, private/locked/full exclusion, capacity including
reconnect holds, one-time credentials, immutable matched identity, cancellation,
expiration during auth, concurrent redemption and real RoomsClient wire joins.
These cover the requested listing/reservation row against the public Colyseus
matchmaker behavior cited in clean-room.md. No throughput or GPU claim.

Batch 273 failure 18: compatibility compute pipelines enforce device binding
limits across groups before any compute route executes. Five regressions in
`packages/webgl2-compat/test/compute-limits.test.js` cover automatic and explicit
layouts, exact limits and overflow, stage visibility, external textures, sparse
groups, asynchronous rejection and recovery. Async render and compute pipeline
errors preserve `GPUPipelineError` without writing DOMException's read-only name.
The unchanged shader readback GPU check passes on native WebGPU and the full
compatibility layer, including 4,194,304 exact u32 results, abort, disposal,
device loss, failed validation and recovery. This closes that readback failure;
other shader and compatibility parity coverage retains its existing scope.

Verify cache bookkeeping: for 128 existing archives and 12 new package outputs,
`tests/result-cache.test.js` proves 1,614 versus zero per-archive stats under cap
and 1,548 versus zero at cap on Windows, with identical retained bytes, zero
archive-content bytes read by eviction, and unchanged
per-insertion capacity enforcement. Shared touches, additions, removals and
timestamp ties/backwards changes are covered. Fresh native metadata comes from
enumeration, with stat retained for special entries and other platforms.
This is a deterministic work claim only; no timing
or rendering claim follows. Details: operational playbook, section 5 evidence
"verify cache settlement"; research: `docs/clean-room.md`.

GPU lease correction (Bug 45, 2026-11-09): `MARGO_GPU_IDLE_MIN` declares a measured
compile idle window, default three minutes and capped by the unchanged hard hold.
The independent heartbeat reads the recorded window. Deterministic regressions
in `tests/gpu-idle-window.test.js` retain a lease at the observed 194.050-second
reclaim point, reclaim at four minutes, and check defaults and invalid limits;
`tests/gpu-lock.test.js` proves acquisition publishes the window. This is a
scheduler correctness claim, not a renderer, timing or full D3D11 parity claim.

## Batch 258 input entry

`@margoengine/ui/input` supplies single-line, multiline and password widgets,
canvas selection/caret drawing and native clipboard/IME editing through
attachTextInputs. The public row is tested by packages/ui/tests/text-input.test.js
and text-input-browser.mjs, including a real browser composition session and
native undo. It adds no core/stage feature or root UI import. The evidence
scope is automated Chromium plus the complete WebGL2 compatibility route;
no human assistive-technology certification or measured rival speed claim.
Research, rival inventories and the public-domain text source are recorded
in clean-room.md. Reproduction commands are in reproduce.md.

Batch 234 caustics: flat/oblique Fresnel flux, RGB extinction, entry and
receiver shadows, seven independent projector cases, 64/1024-map point focus,
virtual slices, source ordering, resize and restoration pass on WebGPU and full
WebGL2 compatibility. Focus errors are 0.000004759 and 0.000149427 against the
unchanged 0.005 energy bar. The NOAA preview and qualitative remote WebGL Water
review show seabed/raised-receiver capability; no equal-input rival pixel parity
is claimed. All affected fast gates pass after the browser-host repair, with
the caustic compatibility cases passing again on the final main rebase.
Explicit finite-field and opaque-receiver limits and the [acceptance record](../water-caustics.md)
qualify this feature claim. No speed or untested backend claim.

## Batch 213 job 1: legacy VRM reading

`rigging/vrm0` reads the real DWANGO Alicia Solid 0.51 avatar into the existing
1.0 runtime (55 humanoid bones, 48 simulated spring joints). Its browser check
requires the converted geometry/skin image to equal the turned original at
every HDR component, and requires expression changes and neutral restoration.
This is a reading/normalization claim; MToon shading is a separate job.
Original licences remain original, and gaze-curve linearization is explicit.
See [mapping and sources](../rigging-vrm0.md), package unit tests and
`packages/rigging/tests/rigging-browser.mjs` on both backends. No speed claim.

Final batch 171 job 3 integration: genuine entry/network records both name da96932cb, packages clean.
The latest-main rebase preserves all concurrent work. Sciviz/geometry/compute/math/core source is identical
to the accepted source; the affected 203 CPU checks, root GPU checks, 16 entry parity comparisons, four
NOAA wind-path layouts/backends and all 25 README runtime examples pass on the combined build. API,

No ETC2/EAC hardware upload, final all-target or timing claim follows.


## Bounded caller-owned Zstd output

The internal shared Zstd reader accepts an optional exclusive destination.
Its returned prefix shares the supplied buffer. A four-MiB regression records
no output-sized allocation, preserves canaries and verifies exact output;
additional cases cover concatenated and unknown-size frames, checksums,
truncation, limits, dictionaries, cancellation and Wasm view detachment.
The previous implementation fails these destination regressions. Existing
allocating callers retain their behavior. This proves storage ownership and
copy removal, not a timing result or final Basis RSS acceptance.
See [the helper contract and runnable example](../../packages/shared/README.md)
and `packages/decoders/tests/zstd-destination.test.js`.


## Batch 134 current-Wasm corpus, hostile input and uploads

The [continuation evidence](results/batch-134-corpus-uploads-memory.json)
revalidates 51 primary corpus seeds on the scalar-approved combined Wasm:
17,908 historical-target decode calls and 2,856 mutations, with frozen
prebuilt or strict specification controls. New scalar targets additionally
pass 32,784 ABI calls and 628 authored-container calls (4,096 random blocks).
These are bounded deterministic campaigns, not complete all-target fuzz proof.

All 1,296 candidate BC4/BC5 images, 131,581,656 compressed bytes, upload to
real RTX A6000 WebGPU textures and read back byte-identically, with zero
validation errors. Each mip/layer/face is its own block-aligned physical
texture. This proves byte transport, not sampled-pixel or public API behavior.
ETC2/EAC WebGPU is unavailable on this adapter: 993 images remain unproven.

Fresh-process memory measurements cover the same 173 accepted scalar pairs,
all outputs retained. Wasm high water is no worse in every pair; process RSS
is worse in 25. Memory acceptance is OPEN. The adapter duplicates decoded
Zstd level storage between JS and Wasm. A same-payload uncompressed control
preserves 12 old and 12 new hashes and removes the diagnosed RSS loss; it is
not a runtime workaround or proof that every RSS loss is fixed. No timing A/B.

## Batch 171 job 3 acceptance

Batch 171 job 3 acceptance: 14,012 tests in the single full verify, 14,010 passed;
two metadata failures repaired (help catalogue and generated notices); all seven affected checks pass.
190 sciviz/site CPU tests, all six sciviz GPU suites and all 25 README runtime examples pass.
The 16 entry parity cases include zero pixel differences and exact actual-device restoration for tubes
and ribbons (3793/3890 lit pixels). All 40 demo/layout/backend cases pass, including the four NOAA
wind-path layouts/backends. Fresh genuine entry/network records both identify c333a7390, packages
clean. /tubes 2533 B, /grids 23735 B, /maths 5703 B, root 50599 B, LIC 23461 B; core/stage 23924 B.
The analytical sink and real NOAA regressions fail old fields.ts and pass the root repair. Numerical
termination/geometry/native-backend limits stay explicit; no speed claim. Earlier job 3 checkpoint
paragraphs and their then-open repros below are historical, superseded by this acceptance.
[Full receipt](results/batch-171-tubes-validation.json).

## Batch 171 critical-point correctness

The CPU streamline solver no longer publishes the analytical sink's zero-progress sequence or NOAA's
false reversing cycles. The permanent analytical and real-data regressions fail the preserved old tracer
and pass the repair; all 190 sciviz/site CPU cases pass. A small periodic orbit and near-saddle invariant
check distinguish real curved motion from unresolved integration. The error estimate is local and the
spatial termination floor is explicit, not a global topology claim. Existing tube/ribbon GPU checkpoint
records retain their measured scope until final acceptance. LIC's +13,032 B attribution is independently
reproduced with identical measurement settings and only old/current Margo colour lookup/data substituted.


## Batch 134 scalar ruling (Scott, 2026-11-09)

Scoped scalar quality now passes 98/98 BC4/BC5 pairs (1,296 images),
52/52 EAC R11/RG11 pairs (690 images), and 23/23 ETC2-alpha pairs (303 images).
All 2,289 old source controls, 1,296 normative RGBA controls and 2,289 new
payload rehashes pass. BC4/BC5 output/source numbers equal report16 exactly;
all 993 ETC2/EAC output hashes and source numbers equal report21 exactly.
The original-source losses remain 14 BC4/BC5, 26 RG11 and one ETC2 pair.

Scott's written "Do recommend" ruling replaces the scalar acceptance reference
with the spec-defined ETC1S/UASTC RGBA8 decode of the same payload: per-file
SSE, new <= old, zero tolerance, every old control exact. BC4/BC5 retain all
98 pairs, both input kinds and every mip/layer/face. The same rule covers
EAC R11/RG11 and ETC2 alpha. R/alpha channel output contracts are unchanged;
ETC2 RGB remains strict. [Full scalar payload-bar evidence](results/batch-134-scalar-payload-bar.json) publishes every
old/new original-source number, including losses; those numbers remain measured.
The reason is the [ideal-fidelity proof](results/batch-134-scalar-ideal-bound.json):
zero added reconstruction error still fails 17/98 old source-bar pairs.

[Gate record](results/batch-134-scalar-payload-gates.json): the single full verify
passed 13,952 of 13,953 tests; its one stale generated-doc index failure was
repaired by regeneration, then all 29 focused checks passed. Lint, hygiene,
decoders/glTF CPU and all three shared public GPU leaves pass. Those GPU
leaves exercise the shipped prebuilt; no candidate GPU acceptance is implied.

The restored report-16 byte fitter replaces the rejected latent source model;
later ETC2/EAC work is retained in one rebuilt optional private Wasm. The
historical reports below keep their original bars and results. Their statements
about waiting for Scott or a then-current bar do not describe this new ruling.
No public decoder replacement or completed batch job is claimed.

The [scalar ideal-fidelity diagnostic](results/batch-134-scalar-ideal-bound.json)
reproduces 17/98 failures on all images and 3/82 on untouched source-size

Batch 171 LIC evidence: `packages/sciviz/tests/lic.test.js` passes validation, immutable
inputs, tiny velocity normalization, cancellation, resource budgets, partial-allocation cleanup and device
reconstruction. `entries-cases.js` independently checks horizontal/vertical analytical box convolution with
absolute luminance bound 2e-6, static sign reversal, anisotropic axes, oblique slices, boundaries, masks,
stagnation and periodic phase. The harness also enforces exact restored pixels and existing strict backend
parity. [Box/slice/animation evidence](results/batch-171-lic.json) has maximum analytical error
1.4725853414443435e-7, 21,681 lit pixels and zero differing pixels between backends.
[Exact circular-arc evidence](results/batch-171-lic-curvature.json) has maximum error 1.1380660922433172e-5,
22,492 lit pixels, exact restoration, and zero backend differences. Reversing phase and velocity preserves
animation luminance within 2e-6. [NOAA demo evidence](results/batch-171-lic-demo.json) covers both desktop/mobile
layouts on both backends, day/colour/animation changes after the initial load deadline expires, and PNG export.
The one full verify ran 13,769 tests (13,765 passed); its four metadata/test-fixture failures were corrected and
the affected checks passed. [Closure evidence](results/batch-171-lic-validation.json) records those checks and
the coherent clean-source entry/network pair. All package GPU suites, 24 sciviz and three umbrella README examples pass.
Rebased main's fixed-wait scanner also passes after documenting the compute-owner test's existing exact
four-step invariant, with its original GPU value/count assertions unchanged and passing on both backends.
The [normalization regression](results/batch-171-lic-normalization.json) records zero error for mixed parallel
speeds spanning 24 orders against the unit-speed image on both backends. The old
squared norm changed 928/2,048 luminances. Permanent GPU/CPU regressions cover scaled norms and portable input
headroom without changing any existing bounds. Normalized LIC was 10,429 B gzip before main's colormap
precision restoration. The integrated entry is 23,461 B, ceiling 24,165 B, and core + stage is 23,924 B.
The coherent entry/network pair measures clean source c1a799e75. No speed result;
[method sources](../clean-room.md#batch-171-job-2-line-integral-convolution).

Owned Windows Chromium teardown reads its process list before client disconnect
can invalidate that read. The asynchronous-session regression in
`tests/browser-teardown.test.js` fails on the prior ordering; all 56 scoped
browser/shutdown tests and all nine voxel GPU suites pass. Existing process-list
and shutdown deadlines remain unchanged. This is correctness evidence only.

Batch 171 CPU flow foundation: `/flow` integrates physical-time pathlines through regular 2D/3D fields with
spatial/temporal interpolation and RK4. `packages/sciviz/tests/flow.test.js` checks analytic constant/temporal
and rotation/expansion fields, backwards integration, NaNs, budgets, exact-reason abort and concurrent isolation.
The NOAA fixture checks deterministic two-day paths and 20 m endpoint agreement after halving a 600 s step;
this refinement result for daily means is not observational accuracy. Raw Wasm is 7,916 bytes, below the enforced
16,000-byte cap. The continuation adds GPU particles/trails, seeding/respawn, streaklines and an official demo
as described below.
No throughput or frame-rate claim follows.

Browser integration proof: `entries-browser.mjs --only=flow-pathline` verifies every analytic sample after
real lazy Wasm loading, renders 1,517 lit pixels with the existing line layer on WebGPU and WebGL2 compatibility,
and preserves all pixels on actual device replacement. The unchanged all-pixel parity bounds pass.
This is a CPU pathline/rendering proof; direct native WebGL2 lines remain pending.

Batch 171 moving-flow evidence: `flow-seeds.test.js` checks four deterministic layouts and analytic unsteady
streakline release times, with exactly one Wasm instantiation/field copy for multiple releases. The GPU
`flow-motion` case checks all 96 constant-field particles against analytic positions, unsteady 3D rotation/
expansion against the CPU reference within 0.0002 distance units, NaNs, respawn, abort and exact replay pixels
after actual device replacement. WebGPU and WebGL2 compatibility pass the existing all-pixel parity bounds.
The NOAA Moving wind demo passes desktop/mobile, seed-layout changes, playback, particle-only drawing and PNG
export on both backends. Its fixed gold pathline and pink streakline are separately labelled. These daily-mean
numerical trajectories are not observed tracks or forecasts. No cross-hardware bit-identity or speed claim.

Test-page 36: mesh position, rotation and scale retain their refs and reject assignment
with a coded TypeError before store writes. Proof: core/tests/transform-refs.test.js
fails on the writable-field parent, passes with the guard, and preserves inspector
snapshot/restore and ref identity through store growth. Core + stage: 23,837 to
23,924 B gzip (+87 B); core alone 21,553 B, below its unchanged 21,587 B cap.
No throughput or cross-hardware claim.

Native GL reboot ownership repair (batch 181): the preview renderer releases its current program before
deletion on live restoration and disposal, so another plugin does not retain a binding that becomes invalid
when switched away. Foreign current programs are preserved. `preview-webgl.test.js` is the failing-before,
passing-after CPU regression for owned, foreign and null bindings; sciviz's strict real-context reboot
checkpoints now pass after main's teardown repair. This is a
resource-lifetime claim only; no new hardware, pixel-equivalence or speed claim follows.

Batch 181 native drawing piece (2026-11-10): `sciviz/webgl2` implements CPU-backed height surfaces and
instanced rectangles on an engine whose actual backend is WebGL2, without a WebGPU shim. The root and the CPU
extractor do not load that optional implementation. `packages/sciviz/tests/webgl2.test.js` checks delegation,
latest-height restore, ownership, resource limits and explicit unsupported paths. The browser test checks
both real engine backends, all-pixel parity, update/NaN/opacity/contour cases, live reconstruction and GL state.
All eight image pairs pass the unchanged all-pixel bounds on Chromium/RTX A6000, including real
WEBGL_lose_context restoration, hostile GL state, live reboots and disposal with NO_ERROR. Complete
missing-triangle rejection and finite one-sided normals preserve parity at NaN holes. The first-view frame
clear also covers pixels outside its scissor. Evidence: resume-webgl-checkpoint-native.log and webgl2-pixels.json
in test-results/sciviz-fixes. No full-job completion, speed or other-hardware claim is made.
Axes, points/lines, volume/slice and CPU-isosurface integration and all three real-data demos are still owed.

CPU isosurface foundation (2026-11-10): `@margoengine/sciviz/fallback` extracts without a GPU using the existing
derived case table and a feature-local Rust/Wasm module. `packages/sciviz/tests/fallback.test.js` compares all
256 cases and non-cubic/chunked grids with an independent float32 scalar oracle, checks cancellation and memory
ownership, and pins the real head MRI at 216,150 triangles for intensity 300. The full scalar and SIMD Wasm vertex
hash is `57d02c9bce60a0d5e342b0551244d6bb12a597802945020f2b62ff60e6475cb9`. This establishes CPU extraction
correctness; direct WebGL2 Figure support and rendering parity remain unverified. No public speed ratio is claimed.

Half-float allocation correction (2026-11-10): scalar encoding now reuses two views over one four-byte buffer.
`tests/f16-allocation.test.js` proves zero per-value view allocations, against 574,992 for a default 66-cubed brick
on the parent, and checks every binary16 word plus signed rounding ties. Selected bricks, samples and output bits
are preserved. This is an allocation-count claim, without a wall-clock speed claim. Reproduce with
`node --test tests/f16-allocation.test.js`.

Voxel streaming admission (2026-11-09): `packages/voxel/tests/streamer.test.js` deterministically checks that
five wanted 36,876-byte stored chunks load only three under a 110,628-byte budget, with one or unlimited joins
per update. Variable-size and real worker replies also respect actual admission bytes; increasing the budget
resumes retained results. All three regressions fail on the previous streamer. This bounds loaded chunk data,
not process memory, pending jobs or subsequent edits; see [limits](../limits.md).

The [active licensed corpus](results/batch-134-active-corpus.json)
replaces the AGI atlas, alpha3, Kodak03 and Memorial cube with CC0 assets of the
same dimensions, input formats, alpha/cube features and mip counts. All 699
served pairs and 9,491 images now have source metrics, with zero provisional
pairs. The four replacements account for 45 pairs and 250 images. Three .basis
files use real FlightHelmet image content; the HDR cube uses an analytic light
field. The historical baseline is retained, and its timings are not transplanted
onto new inputs. Replacement timing and the transcoder's acceptance gates remain.

Biome prefetch duplication closure (2026-11-09): the unchanged full default 4 km world, three
workers and original 144-tile rectangle produce 144 view/144 placement jobs instead of
204/190. Every field and full typed-array SHA-256 matches the parent for all 292 unique keys.
Proof: results/biomes-prefetch-20261109.json, generated by
packages/biomes/scripts/prefetch-proof.mjs. Cancellation, failure, edit revisions, capacity and
disposal are covered by packages/biomes/tests/view-prefetch.test.js. The entry is 10,454 B gzip
against the unchanged ceiling of 10,454 B. This is a work-count and correctness claim, not a
wall-clock ratio or a rival comparison.

Sciviz review corrections (2026-11-10): `restoration-browser.mjs` uses the head MRI with an actual replacement
device, a held active extraction, a later level update and new surface creation, and compares the restored pixels.
It also tests the review's held `[4,4,4]` two-frame source after cancellation and late completion. All root layer
types are audited, and `entries-browser.js` compares the grids, graphs and map entries before/after replacement.
`demos-browser.mjs` measures every visible head/star axis label at 390 x 844 and 1440 x 900 on both backends,
requiring 8 CSS pixels of separation. CPU-backed layers rebuild; caller-owned GPU data has no mirror and must
be recreated after loss. These checks add lifecycle and layout correctness evidence in Chromium on this machine;
they establish no new performance, browser-family or hardware claim.

Real-data sciviz examples (2026-11-10): the MRI page renders a CC0 OpenNeuro T1w volume, slices and isosurface;
the catalogue page plots one million public-domain 2MASS measurements with EDL, box/lasso selection and a linked
colour histogram; the wind page replaces streamlines and arrows over eight real NOAA daily fields.
`packages/sciviz/tests/demo-data.test.js` pins asset hashes, checks interior MRI tissue variation in three axes,
all one million finite catalogue records, and wind signs and temporal variation. `demos-browser.mjs` checks
the controls and downloads, structured rendered pixels, exact selected histogram counts, no empty wind frames
and no duplicate traces under rapid seeks. It also runs the four retained synthetic API test pages, at
1440 x 900 and 390 x 844 on WebGPU and forced WebGL2. Dataset biases, units, projections and source terms are
in `examples/assets/sciviz/README.md`. No performance or scientific inference claim accompanies these examples.

Sciviz update safety (2026-11-10): overlapping isosurface extractions publish matching vertices, level and
triangle count in request order; failed or aborted extractions keep the displayed surface. Bricked frame
requests resolve for their own step. Timeline disposal prevents late index/event publication.
Figure disposal during isosurface creation cannot register a late layer or leak its resources. Proof:
`packages/sciviz/tests/updates.test.js` holds completions deterministically, `sciviz-browser.js` compares
overlapping levels with the CPU triangle count and original rendered surface, and `bricks-browser.js` checks
overlapping steps and final pixels. Volume/isosurface single-slice errors identify the insufficient axis and
point to a 2D heatmap; mismatched value counts retain their separate error (same unit test).

`Figure.replace(old, next)` supports scatter, quiver, streamlines and histogram trace rebuilds without an empty
frame when called immediately after awaiting the new layer. `entries-browser.mjs` checks queued seeks, lit
pixels, one remaining layer and export on native WebGPU and forced WebGL2, then compares final images with
the existing parity bars. The new image also equals a fresh draw; removing the trace leaves no stale pixels.
Removal detaches points and lines synchronously, and attached figure disposal frees those traces (unit tests).
It rebuilds resources and briefly holds both layers. These are correctness claims;
no speed or official-demo presentation claim is made. Reproduction commands are in reproduce.md.

Batch 134's [partial prebuilt Basis baseline](results/batch-134-prebuilt-baseline.json)
records 51 attempted files, 699 successful file/target pairs and 9,491 image
hashes. Built and vendor wrappers agree on those hashes and the 186 measured
base-level source-quality pairs. The [performance entry](perf-history.md#batch-134-prebuilt-basis-targets-baseline-incomplete)
separates loaded-machine same-prebuilt controls from replacement claims.
Extended source quality now covers 685/699 pairs and 9,477/9,491 images with
all 21 served readers; 14 AGI pairs are unmeasured and 12 alpha3 pairs remain
provisional. The manager accepts this baseline to start job 2, requiring openly
licensed replacements for the three .basis files and Memorial HDR cube whose
image grants are unproven. No missing metric is counted as passing.
No replacement, new/old speed, fuzz or GPU proof is claimed.

### Complete biome I/O output and README lifecycle repair (2026-11-09)

The complete default 4 km height PNG, four weight planes, RGBA splat PNG, four mask PNGs,
temperature CSV and saved-world digest match parent 349243b3d exactly. Export tile requests
are 1,024 rather than 1,536; the height-only request samples its exact rectangle and omits
climate/derivative computations. Bounded worker batches preserve all bytes, including edits
and cropped edge tiles, and settle started copies on error. Record:
results/biomes-io-20261109.json. This proves deterministic output/work reduction, not a
wall-clock speed ratio, new ecological fidelity or cross-device performance.

README page lifecycles now respect the run's single GPU slot. Its 20 s deadline and all
example workloads remain unchanged. The GPU mutex regression recreates an actual Windows
pending-deletion EPERM and demonstrates that retiring the name makes a new acquisition safe.

The lane-Q README repair (2026-11-09) runs all 40 examples in React, Stage, text and worker with zero failures.
This closes five runtime exceptions: React CommonJS conversion, a self-hosted asynchronous picking test, Stage's
static glTF selection, a generated OFL ASCII atlas and the worker companion source. It proves these examples on
Chromium on this machine, not every README or cross-browser rendering. Reproduce with the four-package README
command in reproduce.md. Harness and atlas regressions run in verify; the text browser suite fetches the atlas and
reads back rendered glyph pixels. The atlas source, generator and SIL OFL terms are recorded in THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md.

This page lists the numbers and capability statements in the docs, the script or test that backs each one, and a
status. If a number in the README, on margoengine.com or in `docs/` is not here or in a committed result file,
treat it as unverified and open an issue.

Commands for rerunning everything are in [reproduce.md](reproduce.md). Every past performance loss and significant
bug the checks caught, with its cause, fix, after number and guard, is in [perf-history.md](perf-history.md). What each
finished batch of work landed, how it was proved and what is still unproved is in [build-record.md](build-record.md).
The [required-input audit](../work-lists/missing-test-items.md) records fixture acquisition, missing-input
failures and remaining real-file/reference gaps. Passing its gate does not promote a stand-in claim to real-file proof.

Which claims rest only on a validator, our own reader, a stand-in or a built file, and what would really prove them, is in
[proof-audit.md](proof-audit.md).

Status:

- **PROVEN**: a committed script and a committed result (or a test that runs in `npm test`) support the exact
  wording. A dated statement ("measured at X, before Y") counts when its committed result supports it.
- **PROVEN (STAND-IN)**: a committed test supports the wording, but on a stand-in for the real thing: simulated events, a
  simulated session, a forced device or a stand-in canvas or renderer. The code path ran; the real device, runtime or tool
  has not. The row says what stood in.
- **UNPROVEN**: the wording describes what the code does, but no committed result backs the number yet.

Unless a row says otherwise, real-GPU numbers come from one machine:

- ASUS Zenbook S 14, Intel Core Ultra 7 258V with an Intel Arc 140V integrated GPU (driver 32.0.101.8860), Windows 11.
- Headless Edge 155, vsync and the frame-rate cap off.
- AC power checked before and after, with a GPU lock held so no other benchmark runs at the same time.

Some newer results (the voxel sandbox and multiplayer, the create-margo starters) come from a desktop with an AMD
Ryzen 9 9950X and an NVIDIA RTX A6000, and say so. On Apple silicon, only the results that name an Apple M6 Mac mini. No speed has been measured on Android, in Firefox
or in Safari. [docs/limits.md](../limits.md) lists what is untested.

## Smallest core (measured 2026-11-10)

Claim: Margo has the smallest core of the measured engines that ship PBR, lights and a scene graph. It is not a claim about every 3D engine.

Inclusion rule: only engines that ship PBR materials, light objects and a scene graph are compared. Thin WebGL wrappers and helper libraries are not in the table.

Sizes are gzip bytes of the bundled, minified output of each engine, installed from npm and used as a black box with the entry its documentation shows. Row (a) is a minimal lit scene: renderer, scene, perspective camera, one lit box, one directional light, a render loop. Row (b) is everything the package's main entry exports.

| Engine | Version | (a) minimal lit-box scene, gzip B | (b) whole package, gzip B | WebGPU / WebGL2 | PBR | Lights |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Margo (`createStage` + box) | 1.0.0, main at 7c2d18a60 | 24,130 | 24,204 | WebGPU / fallback is a separate package | yes | yes |
| ClayGL | 1.3.0 | 81,424 | 113,711 | no / yes (WebGL1 and 2) | yes | yes |
| Hilo3d | 1.19.1 | 112,039 | 111,842 | no / yes (WebGL1 and 2) | yes | yes |
| three.js, WebGLRenderer | 0.187.0 | 130,100 | 186,538 | no / yes | yes | yes |
| three.js, WebGPURenderer | 0.187.0 | 218,343 | 301,596 | yes / yes | yes | yes |
| Galacean | 1.6.13 | 289,754 | 289,751 | no / yes | yes | yes |
| PlayCanvas (`AppBase`, 3 component systems) | 2.24.0 | 290,840 | 734,363 | yes / yes | yes | yes |
| Babylon.js, Engine (WebGL) | 9.35.0 | 427,200 (startup 292,163) | 1,838,354 | no / yes | yes | yes |
| Babylon.js, WebGPUEngine | 9.35.0 | 497,381 (startup 355,304) | same as above | yes / no | yes | yes |
| Filament | 1.53.4 | not measured | 35,982 JS + 1,969,736 Wasm | yes / yes | yes | yes |

Margo against the next smallest in row (a): 3.4 times smaller than ClayGL, 5.4 times smaller than three.js WebGLRenderer, 9.0 times smaller than three.js WebGPURenderer.

Margo's own figures on main: `npm run measure:size` prints 24,209 B gzip (59,802 B raw) for core + stage, the number in the README. The comparison script bundles the same stage entry with its own copy of the build call and prints 24,204 B (5 B less, from its separate install), 24,130 B for row (a) and 24,204 B for row (b).

Stated differences:

- Margo's figure is WebGPU only. The WebGL2 fallback is the separate optional `@margoengine/webgl2-compat`, which WebGPU users never download. Its full layer is 106,729 B gzip and its small preview entry is 3,533 B gzip (`bundleBytes` from `scripts/measure-size.js`, bare imports left out). Core + stage + the full layer is about 130,900 B gzip added together, an upper bound because the packages share some code. three.js WebGPURenderer, PlayCanvas and Filament carry their WebGL2 path inside their figures.
- Margo's row includes camera controls (the stage's CAD camera controls) and the Wasm kernel, inlined and counted. The rival rows have no controls.
- No row includes shadows.
- Babylon.js loads shaders through `import()`. Every lazy chunk is counted, as Margo's script counts every chunk; the startup-only (static) figure is given in brackets. luma.gl is not in the table because it ships no PBR material, light objects or scene graph.
- Filament has no row (a): a minimal scene needs a material compiled with Filament's `matc` tool, and the npm package ships none. Its row (b) is the JavaScript plus the Wasm, which loads at startup.
- Galacean and Hilo3d do not tree-shake, so their row (a) equals row (b), or nearly so.
- The bundler version matters at this scale. The comparison pins rolldown 1.2.13, the version the lock file uses with vite 8.3.4; rolldown 1.2.18 gave Margo 97 B more on an earlier build.

Reproduce: `docs/whitepaper/reproduce.md`, section "Engine size comparison".

## Speed compared with three.js

There is no publishable result yet, so neither the docs nor the site claim Margo is faster than three.js.

Comparisons run through `npm run bench -- --engines=all`, with the other engines kept outside this repository in
engine-bench (see [docs/benchmarking.md](../benchmarking.md)). The scenes pair core meshes with three.js `Mesh` and
instanced meshes with three.js `InstancedMesh`, at 4x MSAA on both sides, with GPU timestamps on WebGPU and
`EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2` on WebGL2.

The only runs so far were diagnostics on a busy machine (`results/vs-threejs-*.json`, discussed in
[boxes-vs-threejs.md](boxes-vs-threejs.md)). They are not results and are not quoted anywhere as a comparison.

The quiet-machine run, with an A/A run to show the noise floor, decides what gets published.

## Not yet backed by a committed result

These statements describe what the code does, and the docs word them that way, but no committed result file
backs them yet:

- Per-effect alpha behaviour on a transparent canvas (post and environment READMEs). The real-GPU script
  `transparent-canvas-gpu.mjs` asserts it but writes only images to the ignored `test-results/` folder.
- Results of the package browser tests quoted in several package READMEs (bvh, audio, labels, lines, text, points,
  environment, decoders, exporters, physics, controls). The scripts are committed and assert these results, but
  they run on a real GPU by hand, not in CI, and write no committed result.
- Allocation-free updates in tween, procedural and cinematic. The wording is read from the code; there is no heap
  test.
- Texture slot counts per material kind in the gltf-pro README, counted from shader source.
- The sky package's recorded traces, which must be re-recorded after the night sky and fog changes.
- Frame time gained by `optimizeMesh`. Its tests measure cache misses on a simulated cache and overdraw on a software
  depth buffer; no GPU run is recorded.
- glTF export timing for the test model: measured once on a busy machine (`results/exporters-model.json`), so it is
  not quoted.
- The tarball and `node_modules` byte counts in LIM-137. `npm run publish:rehearsal` prints them and asserts the audit, not the
  bytes, and writes no committed result.
- Draw counts quoted from package GPU checks (LIM-135, LIM-136, LIM-139, LIM-141 and the like). The checks print them and
  assert pixels and a bound, and write no committed result. `models-cull-check`, `meshes-occlusion-check` and `lod-check` were
  rerun on the RTX A6000 at `52273e0a7` on 2026-11-06: 0 differing pixels, 393 of 1,600 rows and 9 of 1,509 meshes, as quoted.

| LIM-466 | Graph layouts: `@margoengine/sciviz/graph` lays out graphs with Fruchterman and Reingold's forces (d^2 / k pull along edges, k^2 / d push between all pairs, a temperature that cools to a simmer floor) through a Barnes and Hut quadtree or octree, in 2D or 3D, and draws edges as GPU lines and nodes as fixed-size discs. With theta 0 the push equals the all-pairs sum; with theta 0.5 the 300-node push is within 2 % of it; a cell holding the node is never taken as one body, nor a cell whose mass sits on its far side for a node beside its near side (Barnes's 1994 opening test); a 24-node ring settles with edge lengths within 10 % of each other for ten seeds at the default theta 0.9; a 2D layout puts given start points on z = 0; a 1,000,000-edge, 100,000-node graph steps with finite positions. **Clearcoat through `install` (2026-11-05).** The coat dims everything under it as KHR_materials_clearcoat defines, on opaque surfaces too. Clearcoat 1 keeps 0.96 of emission at normal incidence and 0.73 at n.v 0.25; proof in `scripts/materials-clearcoat-tests.js`, which fails on the unfixed shader.

**Cut-outs hold in every pass (2026-11-05).** A materials cut-out (`map` alpha, `alphaMap`, UV transform, `alphaTest`, `vertexColors: false`) now holds in picking, contact shadows, TAA velocity and virtual shadows as well as the other shadow maps; proof by GPU pixel and velocity reads in `packages/materials/test/cutout-passes-browser.mjs` and `packages/shadows/test/cutout-casters-browser.mjs`. `triplanar` is the exception. `displacementMap` and `clippingPlanes` hold in picking, contact shadows and TAA velocity too, by the same test.

**Transmission adds in linear light (2026-11-05).** Through `install` and `Transmission`, on display-encoded targets too; proof in `packages/materials/tests/transmission-shader.test.js`. gltf-pro's native two-draw glass still adds encoded values on a target with no PostStack ([known-bugs.md](../known-bugs.md)).

## Capabilities worth publishing (work of 2026-11-05 and 2026-11-06)

Source material for a whitepaper: what was built, why it is worth a reader's time, how it was proved, and what is still
missing. Each row points at its LIM rows, which hold the exact wording, evidence and command. Unless a row says otherwise,
the GPU evidence comes from one RTX A6000 on Chromium, no speed claim is made, and every timing is diagnostic until the
quiet run. The engine reference ([operational-playbook.md](../architecture/operational-playbook.md)) holds the method of each.

| Capability | What was built, and why it matters | How it was proved | Still missing |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GPU-driven culling for every kind of drawable | Core meshes, instanced model rows, parts with their own materials, shadow casters per cascade, level choice per instance and clusters of one large mesh are all culled in compute, and core meshes, models and Terrain can hide parts. The picture must equal core's, so culling changes only the work. | 0 differing pixels against core through camera, occluder and row changes: LIM-133, LIM-135, LIM-136, LIM-138, LIM-139, LIM-141, LIM-143, LIM-145. | No timed run of any of them. Non-instanced primitives in `CulledModels`, Hi-Z per cluster, instancing of a clustered mesh. On the WebGL2 layer `CulledMeshes` occlusion stays frustum and screen size. |
| Ray queries on the GPU and in one scene tree | Rays traced in WGSL, a tree built or refit on the GPU for deforming meshes, one top-level tree over a scene, picking of points and cut-outs, winding-number inside tests and signed distance fields. | Against a float64 CPU tree on 100,000 rays, and 123,200 rays at vertices and shared edges; trees read back and walked node by node: LIM-121, LIM-122, LIM-123, LIM-125, LIM-126, LIM-128, LIM-130. | No quiet speed run. WebGPU only for the GPU paths. |
| A complete compute model | Workgroup memory, barriers, indirect dispatch, every atomic, texture writes and ping-pong in node graphs, statements with early exit, subgroup kernels, per-adapter autotuning, graph algorithms and neural network layers that run on WebGPU and on the CPU backend. | A workgroup scan, indirect dispatch and generations of life against CPU runs; gradients against central differences; sequential references for graphs; sizes 32 and 4 for subgroups: LIM-074, LIM-079, LIM-080, LIM-082, LIM-167, LIM-170, LIM-171. | Speed is not measured for networks and graphs, and how much a tuned size gains is not claimed. Subgroup sizes other than 32 and 4. |
| One engine down to WebGL2, without `unsafe-eval` | Picking, context restore and automatic escalation on the preview, native WebGPU's compatibility level as a new rung, XR on the layer, the CPU compute tier under a strict policy, the path tracer on the layer, and a smaller layer base. | Forced compatibility device, stand-in XR objects with 0 differing pixels, all 372 buffer-only corpus kernels equal on the interpreter, every compute case under a real header, first hits equal `raycast`: LIM-109, LIM-142, LIM-144, LIM-146, LIM-151, LIM-155, LIM-162, LIM-164. | A real OpenGL ES or Direct3D 11 class device, a headset, Firefox and Safari, ANGLE Metal. ANGLE on Direct3D 11 does not finish compiling `trace` (over 50 minutes, parked in known-bugs). |
| Fewer passes and shared GPU memory | A package's frame is one colour render pass, shaders fetch only the vertex inputs they read, pipelines can compile off the frame, and BRDF tables, textures and raw mesh buffers are shared per device. | Bit-identical frames in opaque scenes, pass counts, no pixel changed with trimming on and off, counted sharing with copy on write: LIM-053, LIM-075, LIM-090, LIM-181, LIM-182. | The gain on tile-based GPUs is unmeasured; every timing is diagnostic; several rows against three.js are still open losses in known-bugs.md. |
| Rendering off the main thread and off the page | A worker entry runs core on a transferred canvas with no core change, and a headless stage, image formats, image comparison and video output render without a page. | Real worker on WebGPU and the WebGL2 fallback, each thread running while the other is stuck; files decoded by the browser's own decoders; video played back and compared: LIM-087, LIM-091, LIM-093, LIM-094, LIM-101. | Node runs on Windows only so far (Dawn on D3D12 and WARP), and a textured glTF does not load there. |
| Exact geometry that does not fail on hard input | Booleans on exact plane-based polygons, polygon booleans and offsets on one integer grid, constrained Delaunay with exact predicates, NURBS, sweep and loft, seam-splitting tangents, remeshing, sculpting, hole filling, baking, modifiers. | Offsets down to one unit in the last place and turns of 3e-16 radians give closed meshes; tangents match Blender corner by corner; closed-form volumes: LIM-036, LIM-037, LIM-038, LIM-040, LIM-041, LIM-042, LIM-043, LIM-044, LIM-046, LIM-049, LIM-051, LIM-052, LIM-060 to LIM-065, LIM-067, LIM-068. | Frame time gained by `optimizeMesh`: its tests use a simulated cache and a software depth buffer. NURBS has no trimming. |
| The light glTF path grows without growing | Quantized accessors, meshopt, GPU instancing, base colour maps and MASK, cameras and lights as data, and `loadAuto`, which loads only the entries a file calls for and reaches gltf-pro only on a refusal. | Real decoder and real GPU draws, a refused file drawn through the real gltf-pro: LIM-163, LIM-166, LIM-169, LIM-172, LIM-174, LIM-175. | WebGPU only for textures; a cut-out picks and casts as solid. |
| Level of detail end to end | Levels that keep UVs, tangents, colours and materials, built for loaded models too, dithered cross-fades, streamed levels, far meshes that follow their sources, and imposters with colour, emissive, animation, shadows and outlines. | Real GPU checks and a real multi-primitive textured model: LIM-147, LIM-150, LIM-152, LIM-154, LIM-157, LIM-158, LIM-161. | Other GPUs, and the cost per cascade of imposter shadows. |
| Lines and points at scale | Two million segments in one draw, hidden-line display, trails, depth-writing lines, alpha to coverage, picking with meshes in one depth test; Potree folders, surfels, adaptive size, HDR colours, mipmaps, occlusion-aware picking. | Exact pixel and world widths, pixels on a real GPU at 1x and 4x MSAA: LIM-076, LIM-081, LIM-083 to LIM-086, LIM-088, LIM-092, LIM-096, LIM-097, LIM-100, LIM-103, LIM-106, LIM-108, LIM-110. | Potree folders are written from the public format, not yet checked against files from PotreeConverter. |
| An entity system a game can live in | Ordered schedules with fixed steps, change tracking, queries with excluded and optional terms, relations with cascade rules, a bridge to core meshes, worlds that grow on demand. | Results equal plain references over seeded work: LIM-056 to LIM-059, LIM-066, LIM-069. | A growable world's loops cost about twice a fixed one's (accepted difference, perf-history entry 61). A quiet run. |
| Input, cameras and controls people already know | Interactions, local players, gyro, pen, replay; lens, exposure, off-axis, wide-angle and view offset; roll, arcball, gizmos, third person and moving platforms. | Synthetic events on real core objects; closed forms for lens and exposure maths: LIM-098, LIM-099, LIM-102, LIM-105, LIM-107, LIM-112 to LIM-119, LIM-127, LIM-132, LIM-134, LIM-148, LIM-149. | No photometric accuracy claim. CPU cost of the third-person camera is not measured. |
| Knowing what a running engine costs | Frame phases, pipeline compile tracking linked to slow frames, leak alerts, field telemetry, feature scaling by measured cost, supersampled stills. | Phases add up to the frame total; percentiles within 4.6%; real `SSAO` and `Shadows` driven through every level: LIM-153, LIM-156, LIM-159, LIM-160, LIM-165, LIM-168. | How good the measured savings are in a real scene under load. |
| Release readiness | A rehearsal of the first npm release that installs from a stand-in registry and type-checks every entry with `skipLibCheck` off, and the browser suites in Playwright WebKit on an Apple GPU. | All 102 tarballs and 502 entries, one published-types defect found and fixed: LIM-137. | The npm organization, a public repository for provenance, Safari itself. |

## Optimization proofs

Every `examples/package-benchmarks/proofs/*.proof.js` compares a baseline ref with a pinned candidate, the commit where
its claim was made, so later code cannot change the result: `node <proof>` (or `node <proof> <ref> <pin>`). `npm run
proofs` runs all of them pinned, two at a time. The ones marked "current" (the `ongoing` list in `proofs/run.mjs`) are
ongoing guarantees and are also checked against the working tree with `npm run proofs -- --current` (part of
`npm run verify`), from the ref given (the last intended change to their output). Pinned runs build the compared packages and packages/shared at
the pin; `@margoengine/core` (frozen), the proof scripts and their fixtures are today's (except core in the nine
proofs that call `coreAt(pin)`, "Pinned runs and the core of their time"), so mock-GPU timings recorded
before the core speed-ups (`0ed56b9`) re-run faster than recorded. The animator proof has no current run: it compares
every transform write bit for bit, and the animator has skipped unchanged writes since `f33e094` and reads
`options.speed` since `6a1c6bb`, so only its pinned run can match.

**Baselines: golden outputs, not commits (2026-10-27).** A fresh clone of main failed `npm run verify`: 14 of the 20
current proofs rebuilt their baselines with `git rev-parse` / `git show` on commits (`6a958e532`, `2d192a71`, `2cd81c1`,
`2484971`, `b3ed8ac`, `6c20076d`) that exist only in one checkout's pre-rewrite history, and the history scrub before
the repository goes public rewrites every commit id again. A current run now compares the working tree with golden
outputs in `proofs/baselines/` (`golden.js`) and reads nothing from Git: per proof, every value the baseline emitted
and the proof compares with (the GPU call logs with every buffer byte, shader texts, function results), stored
losslessly with its SHA-256 in `<proof>.json` and the values in `<proof>.json.gz`. Each was captured once by
`proofs/capture.mjs` on a checkout that holds the reference commit, only after the proof passed against it, so the
stored value is exactly what the reference emitted with today's core and fixtures, and the commit id stays in the
table above and in `<proof>.json` as documentation. No old package source is stored: only output, which the working
tree must still produce. No reference moved and no assertion changed, so every number in the table above stands.
Three proofs needed more than "store the baseline's value":

- **math-consolidation** calls the old copies directly, interleaved with today's math. Each old copy is taped
  (`tape`): at capture every call ran the copy and a stand-in, today's math called with the copy's signature, on
  copies of the same arguments (aliasing and shared buffers kept) and required the same result, error and every array
  argument afterwards (NaN payloads aside, below); the SHA-256 over all 24 tapes' effects is stored. A current run
  replays the stand-ins and must reproduce every digest. Its text comparisons between old copies are facts about
  `2484971` alone and are stored as such. The digests hold only exactly specified results (host-rounded maths, below).
- **post-stack**'s creative pair is historical in every mode (`5b1fe66^` -> `3319317`, both old); a current run reads
  its stored result (identical calls, with the count) instead of rebuilding both commits.
- **ignoreAgx**'s question "does the reference descend from `eaddd64`" is stored as a fact of the reference.

When a deliberate change alters what a current proof's feature-off path emits, advance its reference as before and
run `node examples/package-benchmarks/proofs/capture.mjs --only=<proof>` on a checkout with that commit; it stores the
new values and replays them from the store. Pinned runs still rebuild their commits from Git; in a clone without them
each such proof prints `HISTORICAL: <proof> is pinned to <commit>, a commit only in the pre-rewrite history` and exits
0, while in `--current` a HISTORICAL row is a failure. `npm run check:fresh-clone` (in `check:merge`, so in the
nightly full run) builds a history-free shallow clone of the working tree and runs `npm run verify` and the proofs in
it.

**Host-rounded maths (bug 17, 2026-10-27).** math-consolidation passed on the PC that captured it (Node 26.12, V8
14.6, Windows) and failed on Node 22.22.1 (V8 12.4) on Ubuntu (Intel N100) and on Windows (AMD Ryzen 7735HS): 21 of its
24 tape digests differed, while every call still matched its old copy when the copies were built from Git on Node 22.
Cause: ECMAScript specifies `+ - * /`, `%`, `Math.sqrt`, `Math.fround` and the integer and rounding functions exactly,
but leaves `**`, `Math.pow`, `Math.sin`, `Math.cos`, `Math.hypot` and the other "implementation-approximated"
functions to the engine. V8 14.6 computes `**` with the C library's pow (`--use-std-math-pow`, on by default), so on
Windows it differs from V8 12.4's own pow (`10 ** -5` is `0.00001` on one and `0.000009999999999999999` on the
other; the sRGB decode or encode of 48 of the 256 bytes differs in the last bit). The proof's inputs came from
`10 ** x`, so every tape saw other inputs, and six tapes stored sRGB results. The engine does not promise the same
last bit across JavaScript engines for these functions (as the procedural and netcode READMEs say); what it promises is
that `@margoengine/math` computes exactly what each replaced package copy computed, on the same engine. So the
engine's maths stays as it is and the proof's digests now hold only exactly specified results: the inputs are built
from exact operations; while the proof runs, Math.sin, Math.cos and Math.hypot are exact stand-ins written with
`+ - * /`, `%` and `Math.sqrt` (golden.js `exactMath`; the old copies and today's math both call them, so a different
expression still gives different bits), and a taped call that uses any other approximated Math function fails the
proof; the results that go through `**` (the sRGB curves in parseColor, srgbToLinear, encodeSrgb, toSrgb and
parseSplat) are stored in a canonical form (tape's `canonical`): each value is first checked to be exactly what the
old copies' own expression (pinned by new text facts) gives on the host running the proof, then recorded as that
expression or as the sRGB byte it decodes. The golden was captured again at the same reference (`2484971`, every call
still checked against the old copies) and `<proof>.json` now names the host it was captured on (`capturedOn`).
`tests/proofs-golden.test.js` replays the proof under the other pow (`--no-use-std-math-pow` on Node 26; Node 22 has
only V8's own) and fails without the fix; it passes on Node 22.22.1 (Windows x64 and Ubuntu x64) and Node 26.12
(Windows x64), and CI runs it on Node 22.12.0 and 26 (`node-compat` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`). The 19 GPU-call
proofs were not affected: their stored values pass on both pows. They still include results of V8's own sin, cos and
tan (core's cameras), which were the same on every host checked; ARM builds have not been checked.

**Intermittent browser-suite timeouts (bug 26, 2026-10-27).** modern:test:browser and its slow-compile run sometimes
failed with "page.waitForFunction: Timeout 30000ms exceeded" waiting for `window.modern`, in check:merge in two
worktrees, with no GPU reset in the window. Under load with another suite running it failed 3 times in about 24 runs.
Each time the page had loaded its modules within 0.5 s, the dev server had no request pending, and
`node_modules/.vite/deps/react_jsx-runtime.js` had answered 504 (Vite's "Outdated Optimize Dep"), so the module graph
never ran. Cause: an obsolete checkout setup linked the main checkout's `node_modules/.vite` into every isolated
checkout, so all their dev servers shared one dependency cache and one re-optimized what another was serving. Each
checkout now keeps its own tool caches; after the fix the same load ran 10 of 10, 8 of them beside another suite.

**Pinned runs and the core of their time (bug 6, 2026-10-27).** `npm run proofs` (pinned) failed nine proofs on a
checkout holding their commits: color-output, environment-volumetric, post-ssr, post-ssr-models, post-stack,
probes-decals and quality-presets ("story must exercise the GPU", "fog requires the standard core PBR shader"),
math-consolidation and water-ssr. None was retired; each now reproduces its recorded result:

- **The seven GPU-call proofs:** a pinned run built the old package sources at their claim commits but gave both
  sides today's `@margoengine/core` and its lit shader text. The old post, environment and tonemap sources patch that
  text by search and replace (environment at `366ebff`: `if (code === source) throw ... requires the standard core
  PBR shader`); core's later bug fixes and clean-room rewrites changed the text, so their stories threw on the first
  frame. When each claim was made, both sides ran on the core of the claim commit. They now call `coreAt(pin)`
  (`proofs/compare.js`) first: in a pinned run it builds core's src and packages/shared at the pin, returns its
  exports and shader text to the story, and every package built afterwards, baseline and candidate, imports that
  core instead of today's (Rollup asks `external` before any plugin, so core's ids are kept internal and resolved to
  the pinned build). In --current runs it returns today's core, so the golden outputs and their checks are unchanged.
  The pinned call counts are the recorded ones: color-output 6,174; environment-volumetric 70 root calls and 344/533
  isolated calls; post-ssr 9,269; post-ssr-models 30,858; post-stack 12,378; probes-decals 15 identical entry bundles,
  135 + 12,320 calls; quality-presets 292/1,141/757/720/408/360 per package. water-ssr and terrain-ssr call it too
  now that they are pinned (below): water and terrain import core's extension entry. `tests/proofs-pinned-core.test.js` checks, with HEAD as the pin, that a pinned bundle has no bare
  core import left and that `coreAt` refuses to come after a package was built (one story never mixes two cores).
- **math-consolidation:** today's script called `mat4.multiplyMatrices(a, b)` and `mat4.invertMatrix(m)` without
  `out`, which `@margoengine/math` defaults only since `f42270c7`; the pinned run builds math at `4eed254`, where `out`
  is required, so the call wrote into `undefined`. The claim's candidates at `4eed254` were bvh's and points' own
  adapters (they defaulted `out`), so the pinned run now calls those, as the claim did; --current keeps calling math.
  On 2026-10-29 its textual check of a since-removed package's vector copies left with that package; every taped
  digest and the current reference `2484971` are unchanged, so nothing was captured again.
- **water-ssr and terrain-ssr** passed no pin (`refs('6c20076d')`), so their "pinned" run compared the baseline with
  today's tree, and water's later intended changes (its foam textures, the ripple noise, above) made it fail. Both are
  pinned to `49f3e971`, the commit that added the capture hook: 2,762 and 6,458 calls, as recorded.

- **splats (found on the way):** its pinned timing failed under load (0.993x and 0.998x against a recorded 2.34x).
  Since `66d1cb30` the stand-in device (`gpu-recorder.js`) made 15 new closures for every `begin*Pass`, about 19 Âµs a
  frame that both sides paid: the 2,000-frame rounds took 43 ms instead of 3-8 ms, and the package's own saving
  vanished in it. Passes are still new objects, now sharing one set of methods; the rounds take 3.3 / 2.0 ms again
  (1.60x and 1.64x on Node 26.12) and the recorded calls are unchanged. `tests/proofs-pinned-core.test.js` checks it.

Only `--current` ran in any check, so the pinned failures went unnoticed for days. A full release validation runs
`npm run proofs` (pinned, about 4.5 minutes on the RTX A6000 PC: 50 pass, procedural-deps HISTORICAL as before) after
`check:merge:full`, alone on the selected GPU machine. It stays out of ordinary focused checks on purpose: about 40 pinned proofs time their claim's speed-up, and the smallest margins on
2026-10-27 were loaders 1.05x, bvh 1.07x and creative 1.08x, which a busy machine can erase. A clone without
the commits reports them HISTORICAL and passes.

**NaN payloads.** `geometry.proof.js` (pinned) failed at random on its degenerate-input case ("degenerate bounds"):
baseline and candidate both return NaN for the non-finite vertex, but V8 writes either x86's default NaN (sign bit
set) or the plain quiet NaN into a typed array depending on the code path and JIT tier, which ECMAScript allows
(SetValueInBuffer may store any NaN encoding). Its bit-for-bit comparison and the math tapes now compare NaN payloads
as one value (`canonicalNaN`); every other bit, -0 included, still counts.

The only normalisation is `ignoreAgx(calls, ref)`: it blanks the `agx()` body only when the baseline predates `eaddd64`
(AgX re-derived on purpose). Re-running the current checks at the start of this work found one real bug, fixed:
`loopSubdivide` threw a bare TypeError on an index past the vertices (geometry.proof.js's degenerate case; now a named
RangeError). Every other difference was an intended later change (exporters' lazy chunks, SSAO's `a281ad2`/`9702e57`/
`e2cc4c5`, splats' orthographic shader `5cf148e`, later edits to the probes' sources).

The nine current references above were advanced to `b3ed8ac` on 2026-10-17 to reconcile already committed, intentional
changes. Each previous current reference was first compared with a candidate pinned to clean `b3ed8ac`, which reproduced
its failure before the render-state work. The earlier core change `6a57389` added ambient RGB to the
frame uniform and made SSAO preserve it; that changes the SSAO shader in the post-stack, post-ssr, post-ssr-models and
quality-presets stories, and the uniform declaration in selection. `2091f12` deliberately coalesced glTF draws, reused
shadow scratch data and changed upload ordering; later PBR uniform specialization and initialization also change the
lighting, transparency and texture-transform stories. `b326518` changed the texture-transform story's normal-frame
orientation and topology descriptors, and `95b4eae` added back-side-only model culling and its selection data. The
texture-transform harness now inlines lazy decoder imports into its single comparison module so modern lazy loading
does not prevent that proof from running.

Three current references were advanced to the rendered-source snapshot `2cd81c1` on 2026-10-17 after inspecting the
complete changed calls; historical baseline/claim pins and every assertion remain unchanged:

- **color-output:** `56ee987` (with `8199756`/`672973f`) unified PBR Neutral display/capture shaders and changed an implicit Tonemap curve from AgX to neutral (uniform float at byte 8: 0 -> 2); 7,734 calls across ten stories now match `2cd81c1`.
- **deformed-shadows:** `56ee987` changed VAT's three shader modules to PBR Neutral; the phone-shadow commits `a126213`/`43aa191` replaced fixed PCSS tables with a noise-rotated spiral, dithered cascade transitions and wrote the pixel scale at shadow-uniform byte 364; every other call and byte is unchanged (147 VAT calls and 319/351/391 shadow calls).
- **environment-volumetric:** `56ee987` changed Fog's mapped core line from Reinhard to PBR Neutral; the root's 70 calls differ only in that shader text, and VolumetricFog still leaves 344/533 external calls identical at 1x/4x with its original pass-count assertions.

The remaining seven current references were reconciled on 2026-10-17 after auditing every changed call against the
later intended fixes. Historical baseline/claim pins, assertions and the existing normalisations are unchanged.
`8199756` is their last display-shader output change; the two SSR stack proofs additionally need `de93aa5`:

- **gltf-pro-lighting-hooks, materials-uv-transform and oit-detached:** `ffd0f05` introduced the shared cotangent frame and corrected its bitangent direction. The lighting story also includes `9210036` IBL multiple-scattering compensation and the earlier phone-shadow shader changes. Texture-transform and transparency pipelines retain `ede41e8`'s zero face/alpha constants (`1fef1b3` only extracted that transform). The PBR Neutral/display hook changes end at `8199756`. Complete traces match that ref: 4,476, 1,321 and 2,732 calls respectively. `e47130d`'s coat/sheen environment hooks were reviewed too; these synchronous fixtures do not activate those physical-layer shaders, so the proof does not establish their visual correctness.
- **materials-uv-transform, advanced to `44592a3` on 2026-10-19:** `ffd0f05`'s bitangent direction is glTF's (B toward decreasing v), but materials meshes have three.js UV orientation and flipY uploads, so their normal maps without tangents shaded inverted (GPU-measured in `scripts/normal-map-orientation-gpu-check.js`). `44592a3` gives normal-mapped materials meshes `+b` through materials feature letter `n`. Against `8199756`, exactly one of the 1,321 calls differs: the normal-mapped box's shader module, by the one character `-b*scale` -> `b*scale`. Every other call, the glTF meshes and the four materials meshes without a normal map, is unchanged.
- **materials-uv-transform, advanced to `fd4d2156` on 2026-10-24 (duplicate cleanup):** gltf-pro and materials now make mip chains with one generator (`packages/shared/mipmaps.js`); each still keeps its own pipeline cache, so the call count is unchanged (1,321). Against `44592a3` only materials' own mip calls differ: its shader module text is the shared copy (the same WGSL, different whitespace) and its single-layer mip views drop the `format` field, which defaults to the texture's format. Every other call is byte-identical.
- **quality-presets:** `4a098bf` deliberately fills light/phone shadow and phone scatter options; explicit values still win. Its six proof readers also include `aa8e8e9`'s slot/frame particle seeds (respawn buffer 8 -> 4 bytes, frame uint at byte 216) and `73102bf`'s minimum-size billboards (projected-pixel scale at byte 92), plus the intended shadow pixel scale at byte 364 and display changes. All no-preset traces and preset/explicit comparisons pass against `8199756`; terrain/scatter are not readers in this proof.
- **post-stack:** `5c047b2` replaced the old two-dispatch Bloom with its downsample/upsample mip chain, bilinear sampler, Karis weighting, averaged strength and output dither. PBR Neutral capture/display and the implicit Tonemap curve also changed intentionally. Its 12 stories now match `8199756` at 14,610 calls; creative's historical `5b1fe66^` -> `3319317` pair is unchanged.
- **post-ssr and post-ssr-models:** the same Bloom/display changes apply, and `de93aa5` makes intermediate SSAO/SSR composites return linear colour instead of `toDisplay(c)`. Full traces match `de93aa5` at 12,431 and 37,182 calls. Relative to the earlier display snapshot, all other calls, resource descriptors and buffer bytes are unchanged. Bloom's HDR replacement was source-audited in that fix; these specific stacks place Bloom directly before FXAA and do not exercise a later HDR consumer of Bloom.
- **terrain/scatter varying allocation:** `957c58b` was checked separately because none of these seven proofs imports those packages. Core's locations 0-3 stay unchanged; terrainId changes 6 -> 4; scatter alone retains 4/5/6, while terrain plus translucent scatter now uses terrainId/emissive/occlusion/translucency at 4/5/6/7 instead of declaring location 6 twice. Complete generated shader diffs contain only those varying-number changes.

**color-output, post-stack, quality-presets and water-ssr, advanced to `780b785c` on 2026-10-24 :** each
changed call was inspected; historical baseline/claim pins and every assertion are unchanged. color-output and
post-stack: Tonemap's shader text only, from the clean-room AgX rewrite (`4a58b5d6`; the curve functions are
generated from one constant table and only the selected curve runs), with the same calls, bindings and uploads.
quality-presets: its volumetric story only (the SSAO, clustered and tonemap stories match): the fog shader subtracts
the view's origin so a fog view can sit anywhere on the canvas, and its bind groups are created by the per-view state
helper in a different order; uniform bytes are identical (`f30bfe1e`). water-ssr: water keeps its foam textures per
view (`f30bfe1e`), which changes when they are created and released.

**Eleven current references advanced to `57250a49` on 2026-10-24 (clean-room rewrites):** each failing proof was

- **clustered-area-lights, clustered-light-hooks, clustered-local-shadows:** 10 of 241 calls per story (shader

- **materials-uv-transform:** 7 of 1,321 calls: the cotangent frame text; on the normal-mapped materials box the
