**post-ssr and post-ssr-models, advanced to `151aa744` on 2026-10-25 (FXAA rebuilt clean room):** `151aa744`
changes only `packages/post/src/fxaa.ts`: the FXAA fragment shader is rewritten from Lottes' whitepaper (texel loads
for the 3x3 neighbourhood, its own contrast thresholds 0.15/0.05, a 10-step end search with an end threshold of a
quarter of the gradient, sub-pixel preset 0.75). Both stacks end in FXAA, so their FXAA shader module text differs
from `de93aa5`; the call counts are unchanged and the full traces match `151aa744` at 12,431 and 37,182 calls.

**materials-uv-transform, advanced to `c640305c` on 2026-10-25 (a material without maps reads no textures):** the
story's untextured materials box (`new Material({color: '#3080ff'})`) now draws materials' bare shader: its texture
reads are constant texels and gltf-pro creates none of its five default textures for it. Against `57250a49`, 1,321
-> 1,296 calls: 5 fewer textures, texture writes, views, samplers and destroys, and one new shader module (the bare
one); every other kind of call has the same count (84 draws, 110 uniform writes) and every earlier shader module is
still compiled. The full trace matches `c640305c` at 1,296 calls.

**materials-uv-transform, advanced to `1fafaf6a` on 2026-10-26 (materials meshes draw in one render pass):** the
materials queue now gives every mesh's `draw()` one `PBRDrawRun`, so consecutive opaque meshes continue one render pass
instead of each beginning and ending its own. Against `c640305c`, 1,296 -> 1,224 calls: 36 fewer `beginRenderPass`
and `pass.end` pairs; the 84 draws, 11 shader modules and 110 uniform writes are the same, in the same order, and every
shader module is unchanged. The full trace matches `1fafaf6a` at 1,224 calls.

**gltf-pro-lighting-hooks, oit-detached and materials-uv-transform, advanced to `b953071e` on 2026-10-26 (the materials
draw work, perf-history entry 22):** gltf-pro now writes every draw's uniform into one per-device arena uploaded once a
frame, shares a 192-byte view block between the draws of a view, shares pipelines device-wide and sets a pipeline,
bind group or buffer only when it changes, and a run's blended draws continue its render pass. Each proof passes
unchanged against the commit before this work on main (`c7a20964`, pinned run); against it the working tree differs
only in those calls, with the same draws and shader modules (first sample count of each story):
gltf-pro-lighting-hooks 2,238 -> 2,090 calls (buffers 18 -> 15, `writeBuffer` 177 -> 23, bind groups 258 -> 285,
`setPipeline` 168 -> 126, destroys 26 -> 23); oit-detached 1,366 -> 1,250 (buffers 18 -> 13, `writeBuffer` 114 -> 18,
bind groups 138 -> 141, `setPipeline` 102 -> 86, destroys 23 -> 18); materials-uv-transform 1,224 -> 1,117 (buffers
22 -> 16, `writeBuffer` 110 -> 39, render passes 52 -> 40, destroys 49 -> 43). The images are checked by the visual
check. The full traces match `b953071e`.

**materials-uv-transform, advanced to `e2cb3815` on 2026-10-26 (steady materials keep their shader, perf-history entry
23):** a draw whose material is unchanged keeps last frame's shader and pipeline, and that kept pipeline makes the
first derivation of a frame complete, so it runs the material's `bindings()` before the render pass begins rather
than inside it. Against `b953071e` the working tree makes the same 1,117 calls with the same arguments (the same 100
draws and shader modules, byte for byte); only 30 calls of frame 9 move: the texture `update()` upload and its mip
chain (`writeTexture`, views, the mip bind groups, passes and draws) now come before the beauty pass's
`beginRenderPass` instead of after it. Both orders upload before the frame's commands run. The full trace matches
`e2cb3815`.

**materials-uv-transform and oit-detached, advanced to `de20fb3c` on 2026-11-05 (empty groups in WebKit):** a gltf-pro material without textures or effects has an empty group, and WebKit gives an invalid layout for an empty group past the last one a shader uses, so its draw failed there. An empty group is now one group of an explicit empty layout per device, which changes these stories' bind group calls and nothing they draw. Materials with entries keep their calls.

**materials-uv-transform, advanced to `20a5e259` on 2026-11-05 (perf-history entry 50):** gltf-pro trims a pipeline to the vertex inputs its finished shader reads. The story's one material with no maps changes: of 1,085 calls, one shader module and one render pipeline differ, and every other call, binding and upload is as before. `packages/materials/test/vertex-inputs-browser.mjs` shows the pixels do not change.

**gltf-pro-lighting-hooks, oit-detached and materials-uv-transform, advanced to `641246d3` on 2026-10-26 (speed matrix
row 12.7, perf-history entry 32):** gltf-pro makes one default view per texture and one sampler per device and sampler
state, where it made one of each for every map slot of every primitive. Against the previous references the working
tree makes exactly those calls fewer and nothing else differs: with sampler and view calls left out and object ids
renumbered, every remaining call is identical, in the same order (first sample count of each story):
gltf-pro-lighting-hooks 2,090 -> 2,056 calls (samplers 23 -> 4, views 34 -> 19), oit-detached 1,250 -> 1,196 (samplers
31 -> 2, views 36 -> 11), materials-uv-transform 1,117 -> 1,085 (samplers 40 -> 11, views 69 -> 66). Bind groups bind
the shared view or sampler with the same descriptor in place of a fresh one. The full traces match `641246d3`.

**deformed-shadows, gltf-pro-lighting-hooks and quality-presets, advanced to `667d1a26` on 2026-10-27 (shadows bug fix):**
the soft filter's blocker search reached the cascade's near plane, `casterMargin` past the scene, and left the
animation guide's robot with bright dots in the head's shadow and a dotted ground-shadow edge (known bug closed; regression
test `packages/shadows/test/lit-faces-browser.mjs`). `Shadows` now reduces each changed map to each layer's nearest
depth and the search spans to it. The calls differ in exactly that, and only in the `Shadows` calls of each story: the
frame uniform is 384 bytes instead of 368 (`nearest:vec4f` appended to `ShadowFrame`), one 16-byte storage buffer, the
reduction's shader module, compute pipeline and bind group, and, after the depth passes of a frame whose map changed
under the default `'soft'` filter, a `writeBuffer` seeding the buffer with 1.0, one compute pass and a
`copyBufferToBuffer` into the uniform; object ids after the new buffer are renumbered. The receiver WGSL of `'soft'` reads
the nearest depth, takes its first search read at the receiver's texel and compares with `min(plane offset, 0)`;
`'pcf'` and `'hard'` receivers change only by the appended struct member. Against the old references the first
difference in each story is the uniform's size and the new buffer.

**clustered-area-lights, clustered-light-hooks, clustered-local-shadows, color-output, deformed-shadows, environment-hemisphere, environment-volumetric, gltf-pro-lighting-hooks, materials-uv-transform, oit-detached, post-ssr, post-ssr-models, post-stack, quality-presets, taa-velocity, terrain-ssr and water-ssr, advanced to `da12bd07e` on 2026-11-09 (capture stability):** core and every package frame now upload the unit light direction and no shader normalizes a light or sun uniform, because ANGLE D3D11 on NVIDIA evaluated that uniform-only normalize an ulp apart under GPU load (docs/known-bugs.md). The shader texts lose the `normalize` and the frame uploads carry the unit vector, so the outputs differ from the earlier references; the goldens were recaptured at the fix commit.

**environment-hemisphere, advanced to `39c3bfe36` on 2026-11-05:** the physical and transmissive shaders' sun lobes take `margoDirectVisible` and gain `margoLit`, so their text differs; the standard and extended shaders, the hooks and the GPU calls are as before.

**environment-hemisphere, advanced to `a1f778bdc` on 2026-11-05:** the transmissive shader gains the surface tint functions, so its text differs; the lit draw is as before.

**clustered-area-lights, clustered-light-hooks, clustered-local-shadows, environment-hemisphere, gltf-pro-lighting-hooks, post-ssr-models and quality-presets, advanced to `6a958e532` on 2026-11-05:** the IBL, sky, probe, clustered and SSR shaders take the dielectric Fresnel ends as arguments, so their text differs. Plain materials pass 0.04 and 1, which give the old numbers; the calls, bindings and uploads are as before.

**environment-hemisphere, advanced to `8d18597ee` on 2026-11-05:** the physical shaders' direct light now scales F90 by the KHR_materials_specular weight and weights the base by one number, so their text differs; the hooks and everything else the proof compares are as before.

**environment-hemisphere, advanced to `b31bdb16a` on 2026-10-31:** without a hemisphere the proof's text differs in one place, the transmissive shader. `tintEntry` gained `margoTintShare`, because the light behind glass is in the base layer under specular, sheen and clearcoat (KHR_materials_transmission, KHR_materials_specular, KHR_materials_sheen, KHR_materials_clearcoat). No hemisphere path changed; a material without transmission emits the same text. `packages/materials/tests/transmission-layers.test.js` has the numbers.

**terrain-ssr, advanced to `ba7a0fc89` on 2026-10-30 (biome look plan L5, item 7):** the clipmap's outer rings
reach past the heightmap, where main drew a shelf of clamped edge heights to the horizon (and cast its shadows inward).
Terrain now discards fragments outside the heightmap rectangle, so with neither the SSR hook nor `roughness` 11 calls
per story differ from `6c20076d` (of 2,269, 1,311 and 4,189), every one a `createRenderPipeline` whose shader text
changed: the colour fragment and the capture entry start with the out-of-map
`discard`, and the depth-only shadow pipeline gains a fragment stage (`outside`, no colour targets) that does the same.
Call counts, bind groups, buffers and every buffer write are unchanged. The proof's stand-in device read
`d.fragment?.targets[0].format`, which threw on a fragment with no targets; it reads `targets[0]?.format` now.
`packages/terrain/test/edge.browser.mjs` checks the pixels on a real GPU (sky only past a corner) and
`packages/terrain/test/lighting.test.js` the shadow pipeline.

**water-ssr, advanced to `268bfc625` on 2026-11-01 (biome look plan 3, L11 and Scott's S6):** against `5cf53d7e6`, 12 of the 2,744 calls
differ, 4 in each of the 3 stories, every one the `createShaderModule` of the water surface (14,445 to 15,191 characters; 14,575
to 15,321 with the horizon). Call counts, pipelines, bind groups, buffers and every buffer write are unchanged. The text changes
for two reasons. S6 is deliberate and changes pixels with no option set: the shoreline term's foam cap goes from 0.08 to 0.35
(`SHORE_FOAM`), so views with a Water shore show more foam at the waterline (76.96 to 111.72 at the minimum channel in
`packages/biomes/tests/water-check.mjs`). The new `still` option adds its code behind `const STILL=false`, as `HORIZON` does: with
it false every added select returns the old value, so `still` off changes nothing else. A water without `still` or `fill` makes
no new GPU calls: no fill texture, no prefill pipeline, the same dispatches.

**water-ssr, advanced to `5cf53d7e6` on 2026-10-31 (biome look plan 3, A2, the horizon sea follows the camera):** the
horizon grid kept regular cells only near the origin, so a camera away from it saw no swell, and it ended in a square
10 km out that high cameras saw below the horizon. The frame uniform gains a `grid` vec4 (the camera position snapped
to a whole cell, added to the grid in the vertex shader when `horizon` is on), so in every story the frame uniform is
528 bytes instead of 512 (its `createBuffer` and every write of it) and the water shader texts (surface, capture,
coastal flow and spray, which share the frame struct) change; the story with `horizon: true` also writes the snapped
offset and a vertex buffer whose outer rows grow geometrically to 10 km and whose last row lies 10,000 km out. With
`horizon: false` the rendered pixels are unchanged (`packages/water/test/horizon.browser.mjs` compares the near field of
both grids on a real GPU).

**quality-presets, advanced to `33c403984` on 2026-10-30 (VFX repeatable particle order):** the VFX update kernel took
respawn tickets and draw-list places with `atomicAdd`, so which dead slots were born and the order of blended particles
changed from run to run. It now runs four small workgroup prefix-sum passes around the simulation, so births take the
lowest dead slots and the drawn particles are compacted in slot order (accepted difference in work, Scott 2026-10-30;
`docs/limits.md`, `docs/whitepaper/perf-history.md` section 6; regression test `packages/vfx/test/browser-check.mjs`,
the order case). The golden outputs were recaptured with `proofs/capture.mjs` at that commit; the proof's three comparisons (no preset,
preset against explicit options, explicit against none) still hold, and no other package's code changed.

**taa-velocity, advanced to `8149bf4e` on 2026-10-26 (speed matrix row 13.7):** TAA's object velocities, a render pass per mesh before,
now draw in one render pass, the PBR snapshots in one compute pass after it, and the camera rows moved out of each
mesh's block into one camera block (binding 4) written once a frame, so a still mesh's block is written only when it
changes. The calls differ in exactly that: fewer eginRenderPass/eginComputePass/end calls, smaller object blocks
(160 or 192 bytes instead of 288 or 320) and fewer writeBuffer calls; the draws, pipelines' vertex and fragment
work and the velocity values are the same, and the parity screenshot matches three.js as before (SSIM 0.641, delta E
2.86, which differed already: the TAA algorithms differ).

**taa-velocity, advanced to `d41cdafd` on 2026-10-21:** a hygiene pass converted TAA's bare Errors.
The story records each thrown error as a `throw` entry (class and message), and exactly three per run changed: the
singular-camera frame now throws MargoError `'state'` (`Invalid TAA camera; give the camera a finite, invertible
projection`), and the two replaced-vertex-buffer frames throw TypeError with the same text. All 7,900 (1x) and 9,023
(4x) entries match `6c20076d` apart from those three; every GPU call, descriptor and buffer write is unchanged. The
pinned pair now names its claim commit `e1a20e13` instead of the working tree.

These current checks still compare every recorded call, descriptor, shader and buffer write exactly against the
committed reference, without normalizing away render-state changes. Pinned baseline/claim pairs are unchanged.
Assertions that the baseline predates fitted shadows, SSR, model capture or x-ray apply to pinned runs; current runs
retain the positive feature assertions, including fitted shadow ranges, SSR/model-capture APIs and x-ray-on differing
from x-ray-off within the candidate. No reference includes the new render-state feature.

Visual references now preserve hardware provenance. The original Intel/Edge files in `tests/visual` are unchanged;
NVIDIA/Ampere with Edge 155 selects `tests/visual/profiles/nvidia-ampere-msedge-155`. Its 55 views were captured four
times from the committed package, demo and asset sources at `b3ed8ac`, using only the updated capture harness and
desktop power probe. All 220 captures succeeded on AC power; 47 views were byte-identical across all four runs.
The [source audit](../../tests/visual/profiles/nvidia-ampere-msedge-155/source-audit.json) records the full commit/tree,
607 verified Git LFS asset hashes and matching default/high-performance adapters during and after capture.
The noise formula remains three times measured noise plus the existing fixed allowances. Profiles require matching
GPU/browser identities; new profiles require a complete repeated capture with clean rendered inputs. Individual
views retain their own source and environment provenance when a profile is partially refreshed.
The render-state candidate, including upstream kernel-column maintenance `ac2fdfa`, passes all 55 comparisons against
that NVIDIA reference with the new controls disabled. `npm run check:merge` reproduces the full build, proof and visual gate.

**Proof stories no longer record error text (DX pass B, 2026-10-26):** a "rejected frame" in the post, colour, taa,
splats, water and quality-presets stories used to note the thrown message, so rewording an error message (messages now
name their fix) changed the recorded calls. The stories note only that a throw happened (`gpu.note('throw')`), for
reference and candidate alike; GPU calls, bindings and uploads are unchanged.

## Pixel batch 102 review fixes (2026-11-08)

Retained evidence: [native WebGPU and WebGL2 layer results](results/pixel-review102.json).

LIM-561 and LIM-562 additionally require format round trips (rgba8unorm, bgra8unorm and back), fitted/scissor intersections including empty clips, and direct disposal of same-name plugins in different scenes. `packages/pixel/tests/screen-regressions.js` runs in both existing scale/CRT GPU harnesses on native WebGPU and the WebGL2 compatibility layer. `screen-pass.test.js` checks pipeline auto-layout identity and exactly-once buffer/texture destruction, including PaletteQuantize and LayerCompositor ownership. LIM-567 additionally checks huge signed finite cycle shifts before small-index subtraction, and coded overflow rejection in both blend modes (`palette-cycle.test.js`). Exact all-off boundaries have zero skipped samples; curved-glass float32/float64 boundary exclusions remain scoped. Evidence is one RTX A6000/Edge, no timing or cross-vendor claim. Architecture maths and remaining gaps: operational-playbook sections 2 and 5.

## Wasm CSP diagnostics (2026-11-09)

**PROVEN (STAND-IN):** policy-blocked Wasm initialization produces MargoError code `failed` with the required `script-src` permission and a help link, including decoder and encoder worker replies. Evidence: `tests/deep-core-csp.test.js`, `tests/wasm-csp.test.js` and `packages/{physics,physics2d,decoders,exporters}/tests/csp.test.js` simulate compilation rejection. These do not establish real browser CSP coverage. [Requirements and paths](../csp.md).

Measured with `npm run measure:size` after the CSP diagnostic: core plus stage is 23,797 B gzip (58,792 B raw), below the unchanged 25,000 B gate. This is a size measurement, with no speed claim.

Height sub-byte accumulation: `packages/lighting2d/tests/frame-regressions.js` asserts 100 directional .001 lights against the CPU .1 total on an unorm target. Owned HDR intermediate, single presentation; no float32 precision claim.

Lighting2d uniform-field preservation: `gi-scenes.js` and `gi-regressions.js` test angular branching 2,3,5,7,8 with an
opaque unit source enclosing every interval. The GPU test reads every float32 cascade sample and requires RGB exactly
one and transmission zero, and checks allocation sizes for every requested direction. Integer direction counts and
constant-preserving bilinear/mean arithmetic provide this fixture invariant; no arbitrary-scene exactness claim.

### One optional WebGL2 package

`@margoengine/webgl2-compat` now owns the preview, optional picking and context
restore, and automatic escalation entries as well as the full compatibility device.
Proof: `packages/webgl2-compat/test/preview-browser-check.mjs` checks the moved
preview entries on real WebGL2, with native WebGPU and missing-WebGPU branches.
This move makes no speed or new image-parity claim. See `docs/webgl-consolidation.md`.
### Bounded Windows browser termination

The shared Chromium launcher asynchronously terminates its owned native process before asking the driver to finish cleanup. `tests/browser-teardown.test.js` requires this ordering, tests an unresolved termination without blocking the shutdown timer, and preserves the POSIX driver route. This proves the control flow, not that every operating-system process can exit within 30 seconds. A process that cannot exit still fails the unchanged bound.

### Scatter tile capacity boundaries (batch 77 review)

CPU budgeting includes the GPU's far-distance endpoint and uses the same f32 tile and eye coordinates with a
conservative arithmetic allowance. Five fresh-buffer regressions fail on the parent: exact distance 4, origin
16777217, camera conversion, a rounded tile upper edge and rounded GPU distance. Each keeps all 64 records after
the repair; explicit `drawn: 32` still keeps 32. Proof: `packages/scatter/test/tiles-budget.test.js`, the existing
check:merge `packages/scatter/test/grass-tiles.browser.mjs` step and [recorded GPU counts](results/scatter-tile-budget-20261109.json).
Generated records on RTX A6000 WebGPU only. No new supported coordinate range, throughput or visual-quality claim.

entry of `@margoengine/webgl2-compat` (mapping in `docs/webgl-consolidation.md`).
The original workspace and umbrella paths are removed before release. Real
WebGL2 preview and full-device suites retain their existing output assertions;
AST isolation and the duplicate-gate TEMP fixture guard package boundaries. This
is an API/package consolidation, with no new rendering or speed claim.

### WFC batch 69 review input and loading contracts

The bounded-heap extraction regression in packages/wfc/test/overlapping.test.js fails with native OOM on the parent
and requires the documented RangeError under the same 128 MiB heap. packages/wfc/test/inputs.test.js covers missing
dimensions, coordinates, adjacency directions/IDs and constraint collections across the entries; sockets.test.js
covers weight division underflow and accepted subnormal boundaries. loading.test.js exercises network/body/file
and instantiation failures, exact abort reasons and unsupported SIMD; worker.test.js uses the actual shipped worker
for missing-asset error forwarding. These strengthen LIM-613/614/616/623/624 input/error evidence with generated
hostile inputs. They make no new speed, authored-asset or GPU output claim.

### Scatter uploaded LOD validation

packages/scatter/test/tiles-budget.test.js rejects positive float64 distances that become zero/subnormal in f32,
strictly increasing float64 pairs that upload equal, and sparse triples. It accepts the smallest normal f32 triple.
The parent accepts the rejected fixtures; all use the owning Scatter constructor, also inherited by ScatterTiles.
This is input-contract evidence; the existing inclusive endpoint, conservative capacity and explicit drawn-cap
regressions and real-GPU fixtures remain unchanged. No performance claim.

## Batch 56 job 2: accumulated planar visibility

The optional staging/accumulate entry samples one disk-light position per frame, sums binary
projected visibility and divides once at the receiving plane. Native WebGPU and WebGL2 compatibility
each match all 16,384 texel sums of an independent ray-plane rectangle oracle at heights 0, 0.25 and
1.3, including a PBR caster case, with specified Direct3D coordinate snapping and top-left ties. Partial-coverage texels grow from
0 to 65 to 371. Maximum 1024-sample empty and opaque sums, convergence, resize, removal, real
device replacement, native fallback refusal, scene ownership and contact-effect coexistence pass. Evidence:
[batch-56-accumulate.json](results/batch-56-accumulate.json), staging's accumulation unit/GPU tests.
The geometry is a stand-in. Equal visibility weights establish penumbrae, not radiometric area/HDR
lighting, photographed-product quality, other-device subpixel parity or measured speed.

### Batch 160: static blend variation below Margo (2026-11-09, OPEN)

The independent raw-API control imports no Margo renderer into its page. It clears
RGBA8, multiplies by fixed finite flat tints, adds zero, and checks every byte of
200 frames in each of two fresh browser processes. GPU completion precedes WebGPU
mapping; WebGL readPixels is synchronous and DITHER is disabled. Both draws retain
all 1,280 selected fixture triangles. There are no texture, PBR, volume, IBL,
tone-map, derivative, atomic or time inputs.

| Adapter and API | Samples | Frames | Unique byte hashes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| NVIDIA RTX A6000 617.42, raw WebGPU | 4 | 400 | 3 (FAIL) |
| Same, raw WebGL2 / ANGLE D3D11 | 4 | 400 | 7 (FAIL) |
| AMD RDNA 2, each raw API | 4 | 400 each | 1 each |
| NVIDIA, each raw API | 1 | 400 each | 1 each |
| NVIDIA, standalone Dawn D3D12 / D3D11 | 4 | 200 each | 2 / 5 (FAIL) |
| AMD, standalone Dawn D3D12 | 4 | 200 | 1 |

[Recorded evidence](results/batch-160-determinism.json) pins every input and outside
report hash. The measured problem exists below Margo's renderer and translator.
A NVIDIA driver/device problem is implicated; its internal mechanism remains
unproved. This establishes no package repair, cross-vendor byte equality, corrected
material accuracy or speed. Jobs 4/5's original budgets still fail on affected
captures, and batch 160 remains open.

or a rendering-speed result. Scoped landing validation and the retained verify failure are recorded in
results/batch-99-guiding-readme-startup-20261109.json.

## Batch 134 private packed and whole-file adapter evidence

The [packed checkpoint](results/batch-134-packed-ldr.json) proves exact frozen
payloads for RGB565/BGR565/RGBA4444: 1,845 primary and 99 supplementary image
hashes. Its block-local output path avoids a full RGBA staging image; this is
a deterministic allocation/work claim, without a measured old/new speed claim.

A private TypeScript .basis adapter now preserves checked Rust container
metadata, image/mip order, native video history, cancellation and disposal.
[Six-file evidence](results/batch-134-basis-adapter.json) records 180 matching
frozen payloads over the implemented targets. These are private development
checkpoints, not public integration, complete Basis support or authorization
to remove the prebuilt decoder. The primary corpus remains 51 files.

[Whole-container mutation evidence](results/batch-134-whole-container-fuzz.json)
checks 2,856 mutations from all 51 primary seeds with 13,507 coded decode outcomes
and unchanged strict specification controls, including RGB9E5. This is a
reproducible bounded correctness result, not a coverage or defect-free claim.

The private [UASTC BC7 checkpoint](results/batch-134-uastc-bc7.json) matches all
345 primary frozen payloads over 26 files, 5,504 crafted blocks covering every
mode/partition/component selector, and 3,072 solid cases in both transfer modes.
The implementation consumes input blocks directly and emits 16-byte BC7 blocks
without full-image RGBA staging or per-block heap allocation. This is a
deterministic work/allocation claim; final timing and memory acceptance remain
open. ETC1S BC7 and public decoder integration are outside this checkpoint.

The [BC7-expanded bounded campaign](results/batch-134-bc7-fuzz.json) adds target 6
to the implemented UASTC paths over all 51 primary seeds and 2,856 deterministic
mutations. Its 14,974 decode calls produced no uncoded exception, Wasm trap or
hard-limit failure. This extends implemented-subset evidence; it does not close
job 3 while target encoders remain missing or claim statistical fuzz coverage.

The private [UASTC ETC checkpoint](results/batch-134-uastc-etc.json) follows the
published ETC hint/alpha mapping and independently chooses RGB selectors by
minimum squared error on the decoded clamped palette. Across26 files/52 pairs
and690 images, every file/target pair has lower original-source SSE than the
old build, using the unchanged stored-channel/exact-area reference policy;
192 payloads are byte-exact and498 differ in permitted selector choices.
Old payload/source controls are reverified, and an independent format reader
checks selector optimality over all crafted modes/partitions/CSELs. Fixed
block-local scratch avoids full-image RGBA staging and per-block heap
allocation. This is subset quality/work evidence, not full jobs2/3 completion,
peak-memory, GPU or timing acceptance.

The [ETC-expanded bounded campaign](results/batch-134-etc-fuzz.json) exercises
all51 primary seeds/2856 mutations with17908 decode calls, without an uncoded
exception, Wasm trap or hard timeout. Frozen new ETC controls were independently
quality validated; existing strict controls were retained. Per-seed input,
adapter and Wasm pins and unchanged source link the result to the ETC checkpoint.
Missing encoders/public integration keep full job3 open.
Review 99 root-glass correction: inverse Snell solutions that reproduce the same
direction contribute one light estimate, paired with one path-side MIS weight.
The root GPU suite's glassfurnace case checks an interior diffuse voxel at IOR

0.02 glass-furnace bound. The previous shader fails at 1.038261 for IOR 1.
Evidence: [root GPU values](results/review-99-root-glass-20261109.json);
reproduce with the package test:gpu script or browser-check.mjs glassfurnace.
This is a generated-scene energy regression, not a new spectral, nested-volume
or performance claim.

to a sloped heightfield, explicit/implicit rectangles and airborne explosions;

The `biomes/erosion-wasm` route supports a 2048x2048 height grid with zero-byte

and boundary cases and a pinned real-DEM crop; the full real DEM's default
32-round result matches independent JS reference hashes and outflow. Reproduce
with `packages/biomes/tests/erosion-dem.mjs` (see reproduce.md). Work counts prove
10 fewer kernel array bytes per cell and four-lane final differences, not faster
elapsed execution. Total backing memory including copies is 244,908,032 B in a

the clean-room comparison are in docs/work-lists/skip-gaps-research.md.
## RT cutout coverage evidence

The optional RT-alpha path tests MASK coverage before closest/any acceptance,
using original face UV/material provenance through leaf permutation. All 379
bvh/shadows and included integration CPU tests pass with independent alpha,
candidate-ordering, overflow and ownership regressions. Real-GPU tests compare
1,020 rays per UV configuration to AlphaRaycaster for closest/any and rendered
filter visibility, including repeated resource setup. The public Shadows.attach
proof uses the complete Khronos AlphaBlendModeTest model, an opaque control and
CPU-derived receiver pixels: default and quarter-turn masks, restored coverage
resources and the map fallback all pass, with zero shader compilation errors.
The finite RT reach isolates foreground panels from the sample's opaque backboard;
the fallback uses its own full-distance oracle. This is deterministic MASK
coverage, not stochastic transparency or hardware ray queries. Reproduce using
the registered package GPU scripts and whitepaper/reproduce.md; clean-room sources
and the texture-rotation reconciliation are in work-lists/skip-gaps-research.md.
Raw passing [traversal results](results/rt-alpha-traversal-20261109.json) and
[attached receiver results](results/rt-alpha-attach-20261109.json) retain the
reported ray/pixel counts, including opaque controls and restoration.

The independent production texture-transform reference passes its baked-UV and
rendered-pixel bars; [actual results](results/rt-alpha-texture-reference-20261109.json)
record the standard-asset pin. All owning GPU scripts and all 31 scoped README
examples pass. One ordinary-RT README requestDevice failure coincided with a
[logged NVIDIA driver reset](results/rt-alpha-readme-reset-20261109.json); only
that failed example reran, once, and passed. The result is explicitly a pass
after a driver reset, with the fourteen first-run shadow README passes retained.

fields. packages/math/test/split-trs.test.js proves the shape, rotation/reflection
recomposition, caller tolerance behavior and rejection of non-finite column
lengths/determinants. This restores usability and the documented failure contract;
it makes no speed or general affine/polar-decomposition claim. Sources and
limits are in docs/size-restore.md and docs/limits.md.

### Batch 273 polygon offset clamp checkpoint

The optional compatibility device now loads EXT_polygon_offset_clamp and replays
slope, units and the signed depthBiasClamp together. Positive clamp is a minimum
with the computed offset, negative a maximum; zero disables clamping. The cache
includes clamp and replays reset to zero. Without the extension, nonzero clamp
refuses synchronous and asynchronous pipeline creation with code unsupported and
a help link before shader compilation. Zero-clamp offset remains supported.
No core or stage source or export changed; their gzip remains 23,924 B.
Device CPU regressions fail on the parent and pass after repair (13/13 device
tests). The complete compat render-state grid now finishes 596 checks and 278
captures: all positive and negative clamp pixels pass at 4x and 1x, including
material mapping. The two high-bit stencil failures remain open for the next
checkpoint; this is not a pass of failure 10 or a suite closure.
Receipts: whitepaper/results/batch-273-clamp-checkpoint.json and local
batch-273-clamp-{before,after,render-state} logs under test-results.

### Video overlay owner and view CPU/GPU proof

Video's optional root resolves its actual scene and applies view state to its
own pass. The parent fails the direct secondary owner regression and the pass
state regression; its pixel repro changes 4608 out-of-scissor components.
All 34 units/guards, six GPU leaves and seven runtime README examples pass.
Each of four owner GPU cases draws 512 red pixels with zero changed components
outside scissor, against the corrected parent's 4608. No speed
claim or whole-audit completion is made.

TypeError before GPU installation or engine creation. Proof and reproduction:
packages/headless/tests/stage.test.js and [size restoration](../size-restore.md).

## Batch 133 development checkpoint (not a release claim)

The unshipped Draco Rust/Wasm prototype matches all 454 original payloads in
ordered native and float bytes. The retained topology suite has 730 exact cases,
including all 31 former capped-fan failures, and the KD suite has 369 exact cases.
Releasing prediction-only owners before final array construction reduces the
same-corpus committed Wasm peak from 23,789,568 to 18,874,368 bytes against the
prebuilt target 20,185,088. Oracle-derived output hashes and the unchanged ceiling
make the memory regression fail on the saved old artifact and pass on the new.
Evidence and scope are in [the checkpoint](results/batch-133-resume.json).
Full feature/lifecycle parity, paired speed, process RSS and final distribution
gates remain open; neither shipped prebuilt has been replaced.

The continuation adds 289 exact multi-parallelogram/traversal cases and 41
metadata cases without removing any earlier fixture. Native and Wasm tests
cover long values, raw names/binary bytes, file and attribute roots and 129 child
levels. The original 454 payloads and both exposed metadata seeds remain exact;
the peak remains 18,874,368 bytes. The package's native/Wasm regression test is
now reached by `verify` and `check:merge`. Evidence is the same scoped checkpoint.
Public text conversion and the remaining feature/integration gates remain open.

[Development evidence](results/batch-133-gates.json) preserves all 454 original

float extractions and descriptors are exact. Native FLOAT64/BOOL extraction is
not exposed by the prebuilt API; its supported FLOAT32 output is compared. The

not a public loader/lifecycle, complete feature inventory, speed or completion

### Batch 273 stencil reference semantics

The full compatibility device truncates dynamic uint32 references to the stencil
aspect's eight bits before GL's signed/clamped conversion. A recording regression
fails the old implementation for bit 31 and UINT32_MAX, equivalent references,
both faces, reference changes and reset. The expanded canonical full-compat
render-state grid passes 616 checks/288 captures, with all old assertions and new
comparison/replace/reset cases at 4x and 1x. This is physical RTX A6000/D3D11
correctness evidence, not a multi-browser parity or speed claim. Native/gate
acceptance is in [the stencil receipt](results/batch-273-stencil-checkpoint.json).

| `@margoengine/lightmap/gpu` bakes progressively on the GPU, matching the CPU bake | `packages/lightmap/tests/gpu.browser.mjs` on the M6 Mac mini (Metal): direct light equal to `./mesh` on 1,024 of 1,024 texels; AO converges (RMS 0.132 at 4 passes, 0.025 at 516); one bounce within 0.019 of the exact form factor | no speed claim (build phase); Metal only measured |
| `@margoengine/lightmap/probes` lights moving objects like the baked room | `packages/lightmap/tests/probes.test.js`: a probe over a floor baked by `bakeMeshLightmap` (sun overhead plus ambient) gives the floor's own irradiance facing up within 3% and albedo times it (plus the sun's known order-3 ringing) facing down within 3%; a lone directional light is exact facing it and matches the order-3 delta-light curve within 1e-5 at five angles | CPU; order-3 SH rings around strong lights (up to about 9% of a delta light at 90 degrees); stand-in scenes |
| `@margoengine/lightmap/denoise` brings a 4-AO-ray bake at least twice as close to a 128-ray bake | `packages/lightmap/tests/denoise.test.js`: RMS error to the 128-ray bake halves or better on a mesh bake and on a quad atlas; flat noise falls fourfold | CPU, synthetic noise and hand-made scenes; no real scene or rival denoiser compared |
| `bounceMode: 'path'` gives per-texel multi-bounce light | `packages/lightmap/tests/global-path.test.js`: a closed furnace gives the exact geometric series for 8 bounces on every texel (physical and legacy); an emitting strip's first bounce on two walls matches a numerical form-factor integral within 5% and 15%, where quad means miss by over 25% and 2x | CPU, hand-made scenes; no Cornell or rival comparison of the path mode yet |
| The denoiser keeps edges: shadow steps, chart borders, creases and ledges | same test file: each side of a 1 to 0.1 shadow step stays within 0.02, charts never mix, normals stop a crease and positions a ledge | edges must be wider than the 5 x 5 noise window's half |
| `@margoengine/lightmap/refracted` bakes rough glass, dispersion and bounce caustics with every watt accounted | `packages/lightmap/tests/rough.test.js`: visible-normal samples match the GGX visible normal density by quadrature (mean cosine within 0.002, density integral 1 within 0.002); the Cauchy fit meets the IOR and Abbe number exactly; roughness 0 reproduces the smooth bake bit for bit; rough slabs spread the beam with the ledger closed to 1e-8; dispersion keeps white light white through index-matched glass and bends blue further than red through a prism; a bounce returns exactly albedo times the deposit |
| `@margoengine/lightmap/apply` draws a bake on materials, glTF-pro and core meshes, and a live GPU bake as it improves | `packages/lightmap/tests/gpu.browser.mjs` (WebGPU, M6 Mac mini, Metal): with live lights off, the lit half of the lightmap shows and the dark half adds nothing on all three; glTF-pro and core pixels within one 8-bit step of the materials mesh; the core stand-in follows the core mesh; `dispose()` restores each; `packages/lightmap/tests/apply.test.js` for the settings and refusals | no speed claim; Metal only measured |

root imports do not evaluate their peers. Proof: packages/react/tests/package-bindings.test.js, subpaths.test.js and
packages/react/scripts/test-bindings-browser.mjs. The browser comparison uses the real MIT Niantic capture excerpt;
limits and the stable-camera identity difference are explicit in the React README. No speed or rival pixel-match claim.

Recorded proof: [batch-265-bindings.json](results/batch-265-bindings.json). Both backends match native line, point, parented line/point, splat and orthographic-camera pixels exactly. The distant LOD contributes 256 green pixels; the outline contributes 1,272 green pixels. The native projection stays on the actual leased camera, preserving same-frame controls and metadata.

### Batch 268 XR mesh pointer behavior

The optional XR pointer entry combines selection GPU rays and exact geometric
sphere contacts for grab/touch. It preserves ordered press/release transitions,
input identity, capture, tracking/session cancellation and mesh-parent React
bubbling. Proof: packages/xr/tests/pointer.test.js,
packages/selection/test/spatial.test.js, packages/react/tests/xr-pointer.test.js
and packages/xr/tests/pointer.browser.mjs on native WebGPU and WebGL2 compat.
This is deterministic interface and real desktop GPU evidence, not a Quest or
phone run. Proximity is geometric (not texture alpha), and per-query world BVH
construction carries no timing or scaling claim.

### Batch 273 round 9: backed texture array capacity

The optional compatibility adapter reports the actual GL array-layer capacity
instead of capping it at the WebGPU device default. requestDevice retains 256
by default and negotiates larger backed requests independently. Core's
existing capacity request therefore enables the unchanged voxel layers 256
and 2047. The full voxel compatibility suite, both slow-path commands, native
bevel reference and five compatibility comparison leaves pass. The native-only
synthetic delay injection gap remains in known-bugs; command success does not
prove that the delay reached the compatibility device. No core/stage runtime,
scene reduction, image-bound change or timing claim is included.
[Receipt](results/batch-273-array-layer-limits.json).

### Batch 197: interruptible camera moves

`@margoengine/controls/transitions` supplies eased setLookAt/setTarget and box/
sphere fits across orbit, map, arcball and trackball. Proof: all 324 controls unit
tests, including frame-rate agreement, roll, antipodal views, perspective corners
and sphere samples, orthographic zoom, navigation limits and promise lifecycle.
The browser suite renders all eight control/backend combinations and cancels each
by wheel input. This matches the four requested camera-controls operations; it
is not a claim of the rival's full unrelated API or timing equivalence. No speed
result is claimed. Research and exact limits are in clean-room.md and limits.md.

### Batch 197: globe controls

The optional controls/globe entry preserves a grabbed ellipsoid point's CSS pixel
through unconstrained grab and zoom, transports its camera frame by geodetic
normals, tilts about that hit above its horizon, and clamps radial altitude.
`packages/controls/tests/globe.test.js` covers spheres, triaxial ellipsoids, WGS84,
nonzero centres, orbit-to-street zoom and an active FloatingOrigin rebase.
The browser check renders the moved globe on both WebGPU and WebGL2 and checks
the projected anchor as well as actual foreground pixels and validation errors.
Reference behavior is the public Cesium controller description; this claim is
limited to this batch row, not all Cesium navigation, terrain or scene modes.

### FSM event payload delivery (batch 250 job 1)
EventMachine delivers the same data object to guards, exit/entry hooks and EventTrace, including ancestor fallback and captured plan actions. packages/fsm/test/events.test.js verifies refusal, payload identity, delayed isolation and exception cleanup. Reference: W3C SCXML event data and Stately guard documentation cited in docs/clean-room.md.

### FSM final and eventless transitions (batch 250 job 2)
FinalMachine settles initial and event-triggered eventless chains, raises completion on the containing state once per final entry, stops at a top-level final, and preserves split-update behavior across delayed/eventless chains. Proof: packages/fsm/test/final.test.js against SCXML macrostep order and documented Stately final/always behavior, cited in docs/clean-room.md. No full SCXML interpreter claim.
### Batch 239 job 1: tile property styling

The optional tiles/style entry evaluates color, show and pointSize from feature properties, including first-match conditions, nonrecursive defines, vectors and regular expressions. It binds to existing PBR model, instance and point rendering APIs; a renderer decorator follows streaming creation and disposal. Proof: packages/tiles/tests/style.test.js and the `style` case of packages/tiles/tests/tiles-browser.mjs, using the real CC0 FeatureIdAttributeAndPropertyTable sample. The claim covers the visual-property row, not vector label/billboard styling or the separate feature/property texture jobs. No performance result is claimed.

### FSM invoked task lifetimes (batch 250 job 3)
InvokeMachine starts promise services on stable entry, aborts on actual exit/disposal, and delivers done/error output without stale-generation events. Proof: packages/fsm/test/invoke.test.js covers resolve, reject, sync throw, payloads, re-entry, common ancestors, transient states, multiple tasks, external abort, preabort and handler rejection. Public reference: Stately promise actor lifetime and SCXML invoke cancellation; citations in docs/clean-room.md. This is promise-task parity for the batch row, not a full actor system claim.

Claim: compatibility canvas presentation supports both XR-to-desktop and
desktop-to-XR device source transitions without attempting to change the
canvas's context mode. Proof: packages/webgl2-compat/test/canvas-present.test.js

## Batch 252 job 1: ground queries

`NavMeshQuery` in `@margoengine/navmesh/queries` covers the web rival's documented walkability ray and local surface movement behaviors. Evidence: `packages/navmesh/test/queries.test.js` checks exact hit fraction/edge/normal for both windings, connected corridors, vertex ties, blocking costs and filters, forbidden/off-mesh starts, off-mesh-link exclusion, wall sliding, sloped height projection and live tiled rebuilds. Contract comparison: Detour/recast-navigation-js expose a surface ray and constrained local movement with visited polygons; Margo supplies those results and a projected move height. This is a behavior comparison against published API contracts, not a runtime speed or universal navigation claim.

The optional sciviz/netcdf reader supports CDF-1/2 metadata and lazy hyperslabs; sciviz/vtk adapts the existing

`behavior/inspect` mounts a complete collapsible tree with per-node last-tick
status, idle for unvisited nodes and utility score vectors from tree.trace.

resume, AbortSignal and disposal clean up panel-local work. DOM proof is
packages/behavior/tests/inspect.test.js; visual usability on large production
trees has not been established.
### Live FSM configuration view (batch 250 job 4)
MachineView draws active hierarchy paths, parallel region paths, remembered state and bounded recent transitions in a live Inspector-compatible overlay. packages/fsm/test/inspect.test.js checks the actual Inspector shadow mount, filtering, cyclic payload safety, text escaping, live refresh and abort disposal. packages/fsm/test/inspect-browser.mjs checks rendered placement, live changes and removal in Chromium. Reference: Harel, Shneiderman and Stately public inspection descriptions in docs/clean-room.md; no editing or complete Stately feature claim.


### Save OPFS atomic replacement (batch 255 job 3)

The optional save/opfs adapter supports binary/empty values, sorted keys, namespace isolation and path-safe Unicode keys. A write commits through the File System Standard's temporary-file close swap. Proof: packages/save/tests/opfs.test.js injects open, write and close faults and precommit aborts while checking exact previous bytes and hidden placeholders. packages/save/tests/browser/safe-check.mjs checks real OPFS persistence across reload and aborted temporary writes. No cross-key, cross-tab ordering, physical power-loss or performance claim is made.
### Batch 197 orbit collision

The optional controls/collide entry retracts orbit/map cameras before selected triangle blockers, retains input distance and extends after clearance. Evidence: packages/controls/tests/collide.test.js covers moving blockers, off-centre corner hits, filters, transforms, input, final-pose events, query failure and disposal; collide-browser.js checks visible target pixels and restored distance on WebGPU and WebGL2. Five samples do not claim continuous swept-volume coverage.


## Batch 252 job 2: partial paths and Crowd

The optional queries surface returns the precise nearest reachable point and a partial flag for unreachable goals, and Crowd follows it with an inspectable partial flag. `packages/navmesh/test/partial.test.js` proves disconnected islands, forbidden areas, outside goals, geometry-based nearest selection, directed jump links, ordinary full routes, tiled rebuilds, arrival and reopening a route. Compared with the published Detour partial/corridor contract, the endpoint is selected from reachable polygon geometry rather than a polygon center. No optimal continuous weighted-path or speed claim is made.


### Batch 171 derived-flow numerical contract

The optional /derive entry computes J, divergence, curl/magnitude, rotation-dominance Q and the middle
eigenvalue of S^2+Omega^2 on a Cartesian grid. CPU analytical stencils, solid rotation/shear, degenerate
and rotated eigenvalues, scale/cancellation, masks and ownership are independently tested. A NumPy
eigvalsh black-box oracle checks 24 own integer nonsymmetric Jacobians, without reading its source.
Real GPU tests compare every value on both backends within the explicit absolute bounds in limits.md,
including masks, FTZ and volume/isosurface integration. The NOAA example is a planar daily diagnostic;
no measured atmospheric vortex, original-data error, material coherence, pressure or speed claim.
Sources and synthesis: clean-room.md; reproduction: reproduce.md. Final acceptance is recorded separately.

Batch 171 job 4 is an unlanded implementation checkpoint: [numerical GPU evidence](results/batch-171-derive-gpu.json) and [complete checkpoint receipt](results/batch-171-derive-validation.json). All integrated mathematical checks pass, but shared Chromium process/stdio teardown blocks acceptance. No full-green verify or completed-job claim.
### Sprite slice and tiling correctness (batch 245 job 1)
The optional sprites/slice entry keeps source corners fixed, stretches or repeats
middle regions, clips final repeats to target dimensions and scrolls atlas fills.
Proof: packages/sprites/tests/slice.test.js and slice-check.mjs. Instances stay
bounded by nine per image, regardless of repeat count. This is a deterministic
work-count claim, not a measured speed claim. Core/stage are unchanged.


### Authenticated safe saves (batch 255 job 4)

save/signed binds format, physical key and complete generation/payload frame with WebCrypto HMAC-SHA-256, then delegates redundant-copy recovery to safeStorage. Tests in packages/save/tests/signed.test.js independently recompute stored tags with node:crypto, flip every stored byte, swap roles/keys, reject wrong keys and CRC-valid unsigned edits, and observe cancellation during crypto. The real browser safe-check.mjs checks IndexedDB persistence across reload and recovers the trusted older copy after tampering. This is integrity with an application-managed shared secret, not encryption or rollback protection.
### Batch 197 bounding-box editing

The optional BoxGizmo provides eight corner scale and twelve edge rotation handles on both rendering backends. CPU evidence in packages/controls/tests/box-gizmo.test.js covers exact transformed bounds, snapping, opposite-corner/centre pivots, nonuniform parents, all three local rotation axes, pointer capture, cancellation and cleanup. The matching browser fixture checks cyan/yellow handle pixels, overlay triangles, scale and edge rotation on WebGPU/WebGL2. This is a mesh-bounds editor, with no hierarchy or deformed-mesh bounds claim.

## Batch 252 job 3: surface sampling and walls

`packages/navmesh/test/sampling.test.js` proves reproducible random streams, the 1:9 area frequency on unequal islands, triangle centroids, slope surface-area weighting, strict-radius support and disk second moments, disconnected-floor exclusion, filters, exact wall distances/normals and partially open tile seam intervals. These match the published web navigation query behaviors; strict radius support and projected heights are explicit Margo guarantees. Distribution checks use fixed seeds and analytic expectations, not timed performance assertions.
## Fuzzy decisions (batch 251 job 3)

`behavior/fuzzy` adds designer-serializable sets, named propositions, AND/OR/NOT,
very/fairly hedges, weighted multi-output Mamdani rules and independent FuzzySystem
evaluation. Outputs support sampled centroid, sampled mean-of-max and weighted
representative max-average. Analytical shape boundaries, clipped rectangular
centroids, maxima plateaus, discrete outputs, rule aggregation and agent isolation
are checked by packages/behavior/tests/fuzzy.test.js. This proves the fuzzy-set,
rule and defuzzification row against published formulas and the documented web
reference; it makes no full-library or real-game decision-quality claim.
### SCXML configuration conversion (batch 250 job 5)
The optional importer maps the supported W3C configuration subset to validated MachineDocument objects without executing content; the exporter preserves native field kinds through explicit namespace extensions and offers a plain common-subset mode. Proof: packages/fsm/test/scxml.test.js covers standard hierarchy/history/parallel documents, live behavior after loading, full native field round trips, stable transition order, hostile/unsupported inputs, prototype-like names and bounds. The browser check exercises default DOMParser. Accepted differences and excluded processor features are listed in docs/limits.md; no full SCXML interpreter claim.

## Behavior tree text (batch 251 job 4)

`behavior/text` parses compact readable syntax into ordinary JSON tree documents
and prints them canonically. All existing composites, decorators, leaves, utility
options, weights and labels round trip. With explicit subtree registrations,
references also round trip and execute through SubtreeTree. Tests in
packages/behavior/tests/text.test.js cover all node types, 200 generated trees,
syntax location errors, semantic paths, comments and bounded hostile inputs.

### 2D rigging correctness (batch 245 job 2)
The optional sprites/skeletal entry supports analytic two-bone IK and bounded
chain IK, slot attachment swaps, discrete draw-order keys and cubic Bezier clip
segments. Tests cover reflected/anisotropic ancestors, bend direction, unreachable
targets, mixing and backwards timeline seeks. Skin-data regressions reject
non-finite weights and fractional indices, preserve bone 65536 and normalize
large finite weights without float32 overflow. Proof: rigging.test.js and the
rendered overlapping-slot/IK fixture rigging-check.mjs. No speed claim.


## Batch 252 job 4: CPU worker SIMD build

packages/navmesh/test/wasm.test.js runs real worker threads behind the browser Worker messaging contract. Whole meshes and all tiled state bytes equal the TypeScript builders for floors, ramps, obstacles, area tags and irregular geometry; retained tiled geometry still rebuilds identically. The reference clipper and kernel produce identical candidate/intersection counts. Per intersection, scalar XYZ interpolation requires nine sub/mul/add instructions; SIMD XY plus scalar Z requires three vector and three scalar arithmetic instructions with unchanged lane order. This proves reduced arithmetic instruction work, not a wall-clock speedup. Startup, invalid indices/options, loading errors, abort and hard-timeout termination are covered; native crate tests cover clipping and span merging. Browser bundling emits a standalone classic worker and separate Wasm asset; rendering is not involved.
### Batch 197 product presentation

controls/presentation supplies bounded spring product rotation with local hit/global dragging, polar zoom and snap-back; native and wheel scroll progress with helper intervals; and scroll/imperative camera or mesh curve following. CPU evidence: packages/controls/tests/presentation.test.js covers frame-rate equality, bounds with bouncy springs, zero-delta finite output, loop seams, browser-default preservation, DOM cleanup, parent transforms and curve endpoints. presentation-browser.js checks changed product pixels, snap-back, actual DOM scroll extent, path endpoint and focused target pixels on both backends. No sensor, performance or deformed-mesh claim.
## Trigger regions (batch 251 job 5)

`behavior/triggers` adds sphere and axis-aligned box regions with point or
bounding-sphere contacts. TriggerSystem returns deterministic enter/stay/exit
events, writes per-agent typed blackboard memory, handles region movement,
disabling, deletion, agent removal and final exit on disposal. Shared boards
separate numeric/string ids and arbitrary system namespaces. Tests in
packages/behavior/tests/triggers.test.js cover published geometric predicates,
finite extreme coordinates, lifecycle, atomic input validation and namespace
collision regression. This covers the documented web-reference contact row and
adds transition memory; no continuous-motion or production-scale speed claim.
### Tween completion and cancellation (batch 215)

The optional tween/async entry offers awaitable scalar, array, path and custom operations and timelines; AbortSignal cancels future writes and preserves its reason. Evidence: packages/tween/test/async.test.js, including replacement, repeat/yoyo, pause/replay, disposal and propagated errors. Public behaviour comparison: GSAP Timeline.then and Motion animate completion controls, linked in clean-room.md. New entry 3431 B gzip; no core/stage source change.

### Object and vector tween channels (batch 215 job 2)

Optional tween/props supplies to, from, fromTo, relative arithmetic and property/keyframe lists, matching the requested GSAP-style numeric-object row. Analytic results for actual Vector3, mixed channels, offsets, easing and repeat/yoyo are asserted in packages/tween/test/props.test.js. Atomic invalid-input and exact cancellation tests cover lifecycle behaviour. Entry: 3223 B gzip.

### Hierarchical mixed tween timelines (batch 215 job 3)

Optional tween/timeline supplies mixed scalar/array/path/custom clips and nested independent speed/repeat/yoyo clocks, covering the requested GSAP/anime nesting row. packages/tween/test/full-timeline.test.js proves overlapping start capture, merged callback order and sample values, repeat overshoot equivalence, cycles/parent ownership, reverse seeks, exact aborts, replay promises and error propagation. Entry 3184 B gzip; no speed result claimed.

### Target/key tween control (batch 215 job 4)

Optional tween/control supplies target/key kills and overwrite, per-operation pause/play/speed and a world-wide time scale, meeting the requested control row. packages/tween/test/control.test.js verifies atomic replacement, delayed/paused registration, remaining-key independence, exact cancellation and grouped callbacks. New entry 3714 B gzip; no core/stage change or performance claim.

### Scroll pin and idle snap (batch 215 job 5)

Optional tween/scroll-pin pins a section during timeline scrubbing and snaps real scroll offsets to nearest/directional labels or other normalized stops after idle, matching the requested ScrollTrigger row. packages/tween/test/scroll-pin.test.js covers deterministic selection and lifecycle. scroll-pin-browser.mjs asserts real window and element geometry, refresh, label/interval destinations and restoration. Entry 2105 B gzip; no speed claim.

### Release velocity and inertia (batch 215 job 6)

Optional tween/inertia carries numeric properties using measured velocity and stops on an exact point/list/function endpoint or range, meeting the requested GSAP inertia row. packages/tween/test/inertia.test.js proves analytic free decay, identical physical samples under delta partitioning, velocity continuity, hard/bouncy bounds, exact completion, stale samples and lifecycle behaviour. Entry 2778 B gzip; no timing or speed claim.
Batch 253 vector input claim: named 2D actions preserve analog direction with a continuous radial deadzone, normalized WASD diagonals, rebindable scalar channels, contexts and tick-exact binding replay. Proof: packages/input/test/vector.test.js. Public rival behaviour comparison and research: docs/clean-room.md, Batch 253 job 1. No speed or hardware-coverage claim.

## Batch 262: standalone tweak panel

The optional inspector/tweak entry provides the binding kinds in the batch row:
numbers with bounds/step, strings, booleans, nested folders, RGB(A), vectors,
label/value enums and function buttons. Comparison is to the public lil-gui and
Tweakpane binding descriptions cited in clean-room.md, not full API compatibility.
Undo groups live input gestures and shares an EditHistory with the inspector; an
arbitrary function is undoable only with its supplied inverse.
Proof: packages/inspector/test/tweak.test.js plus native browser input over a
rendering WebGPU engine and WebGL2 preview engine in parity-browser.mjs, plus
the same WebGPU path forced through WebGL2 compatibility with --compat.
No speed result is claimed.


## Batch 252 job 5: shaped tile obstacles

The optional obstacle entry supports the web tile-cache row's upright cylinders and Y-rotated boxes alongside existing axis boxes. packages/navmesh/test/obstacles.test.js compares every tile mesh byte-for-byte against independent brute cell-center masks for cylinders and box headings 0, +/-pi/2, pi, 0.37 and -2.1. It checks open footprint AABB corners, moving between shape kinds, removal, ordinary-box interoperability, vertical exclusion, untouched tiles, subcell empty masks, atomic invalid replacements and disposal. The near-quarter-turn test fails with inverse-coefficient division and passes with monotone integer searches. No speed claim is made.
Batch 253 XR actions claim: source-local buttons, axes, select, squeeze/grip, grip pose and hysteretic hand pinch feed ordinary action bindings and tick replay. Source loss, session end, missing pose and disposal clear published controls. Proof: packages/input/test/xr.test.js; design and public rival comparison: docs/clean-room.md Batch 253 job 2. No physical-headset or rendering claim.


## Batch 262: viewport selection gizmo

inspector/gizmo reuses controls/transform for move, rotate and scale in world or
local space, with snaps and focus-scoped shortcuts. It follows inspector selection
and shares its history, records one command per drag, cancels without an edit,
and restores the camera's prior enabled state. Capture-phase handle presses do
not become inspector canvas picks. Browser proof drags actual handles, checks
rendered axis pixels and exact transform undo/redo on native WebGPU, WebGL2
preview and forced WebGL2 compatibility. CPU regressions cover cancellation,
recycled slots, abort, toolbar and owner-scene disposal. No speed claim is made.

Batch 254 stereo pan: @margoengine/audio/pan uses native StereoPannerNode for non-positional mono/stereo sounds. CPU graph/lifecycle tests and real browser Web Audio offline rendering check the six endpoint/centre cases against W3C equations. Device output and listening quality unmeasured.

### Batch 197 device orientation camera

The optional device-orientation entry converts standard device poses into a roll-aware Y-up camera frame, including screen rotation, relative recentering, yaw offset and quaternion smoothing. packages/controls/tests/device-orientation.test.js checks 120 poses against independent scalar matrix transforms, exact cardinal directions, finite vertical views, frame-rate agreement, nullable samples and permission lifecycles. device-orientation-browser.js replays native DOM events and checks north/west target pixels and listener cleanup on WebGPU and WebGL2. Evidence is deterministic replay rather than physical sensor accuracy or OS-dialog interaction.



### Batch 171 job 6: atmosphere in a geographic frame

sciviz/atmosphere adds a date-driven solar globe using sky's existing Hillaire tables and transport, including an atmospheric limb, per-fragment aerial perspective and optional density shells. Geographic wind remains GPU-resident after a one-time physical-unit conversion; scalar meshes and physical-size point glyphs use the existing WGS84 projection at metre heights. The source/manifests and numerical/GPU tests are named in the operational playbook and reproduce.md. Existing entries have no static sky import. This claim includes the documented polar-stretch approximation and perspective/exterior camera domain, not exact ellipsoidal or spectral radiometry, meteorological altitude inference or a performance result.


### UI image widget (batch 258 job 2)
The optional ui/image entry draws pixel textures and atlas cells with contain/cover/fill, tint and fixed-corner nine-slice borders. Claim scope is the job row, not all sprite-animation or image-editing functionality. CPU geometry/gutter/copy regressions and image-browser.mjs support the claim; the browser driver checks actual pixels on WebGPU, complete compatibility and preview GL. It also requires zero uploads on an unchanged image-only second frame. No elapsed-speed number is claimed.


## Batch 252 job 6: effective border padding

packages/navmesh/test/border.test.js proves borderSize allocates empty raster columns on all sides, leaves world-space output polygons intact, increases tile context above the automatic minimum, crops output to tile bounds, preserves cross-tile paths and rebuilds, rounds fractions upward and rejects padded budgets before allocation. The worker parity fixture also covers borderSize=3. This implements the documented heightfield-border behavior and preserves the larger clearance context needed by the existing filters.
Batch 253 pointer lock claim: native acquire/release and per-tick locked relative motion feed ordinary Input actions. Promise/event browser APIs, raw negotiation, activation failures, bounded cancellation and modern late-acquisition cleanup are covered by packages/input/test/lock.test.js. Rendered action-to-position proof on WebGPU and WebGL2 compatibility: packages/input/test/input-browser.mjs. Research/rival descriptions: docs/clean-room.md Batch 253 job 3. No physical-device raw-fidelity claim.
Batch 255 job 2: optional save/scene-state binds exact playback and light/shadow state, with owner APIs in animation and animator. Proof: packages/animator/test/playback-state.test.js compares interrupted fade continuation and sync-phase transitions; packages/save/tests/scene-state.test.js proves detached settings and rollback; packages/save/tests/browser/engine-check.mjs compares restored layered playback pixels. No rival speed or hardware coverage claim.


## Batch 262 scene-edit row

Inspector/edit implements primitive/light creation, subtree deletion and duplication,
and tree drag reparenting with shared undo, compared to the public editor/inspector
behaviors cited in clean-room.md. The six CPU scenarios in edit.test.js cover all ten
primitive kinds, inherited ambient, subtree/material sharing, cycles, drag/shortcuts,
resource release on clear/eviction/redo abandonment, and prior property/gizmo history
across delete/restore. edit-browser.mjs exercises real browser drag and rendering on
WebGPU and preview; compatibility runs use the same scene. Model lifecycle and preview
lighting limits are explicit in limits.md. No universal editor or speed parity claim.

The invalid-finalizer regression fails against the unvalidated implementation and
proves rejection preserves pending redo and retained resources.

Batch 262 React material proof uses current attached plugin identities. Historical name reuse cannot satisfy readiness; the native after-scene test still requires exactly one material installation and a visible transmission pixel change. Regression: packages/react/tests/plugin-state.test.js.

Batch 254 binaural: optional audio/binaural decodes first/second/third-order ACN/SN3D to native HRTFs with full world field rotation and listener orientation. A 32-point weighted sphere reconstructs all 16 modes in CPU tests. Real browser Web Audio offline rendering compares the production graph to separately computed polynomial speaker signals for all orders and yaw rotations. The rain stimulus is CC BY 4.0 (audio fixture NOTICE); added HOA signals are analytic test tones. No listening, device-output or perceptual-quality claim.
Batch 257 job 1: optional @margoengine/worker/rpc provides typed promise calls and a sized cancellable FIFO worker pool, input/output ownership transfer and clone/error/disposal handling. Proof: packages/worker/tests/rpc.test.js (real workers and channels), rpc-types.ts (compile checks), browser-check.mjs (browser worker pool, sums and detached/returned buffers). No speed claim or change to core/stage.

The optional xr/locomotion entry adds planar smooth motion, smooth yaw and a movement-driven per-eye angular vignette. These checks establish rendering and input behavior, not sickness mitigation or headset validation.

Batch 257 job 2: @margoengine/worker/auto runs one shared scene module on a supported worker and otherwise the page, choosing before canvas transfer. Batch 254 voices: optional audio/voices caps physical sources across registered sounds, ranks authored priority and estimated audibility, virtualizes silent sources, and resumes them at their continuously advanced buffer phase. CPU tests cover cap=0, ties, distance, loops, seeks, pause, pitch and natural completion. Real browser Web Audio offline rendering compares two priority handoffs, including a virtual octave-up voice, against independently scheduled native source PCM. Device-output and listening quality unmeasured.

The optional sprites/pick entry picks the top image in z/y/add order using
box bounds or source alpha, accounting for camera, pixel snapping, rotation,

centres over four sorting/mask combinations on both GPU backends. Custom
fragment effects are not executed on the CPU. No speed claim.
### CSS3D per-pixel blending (batch 260 job 2)
html/blend's HTMLBlend provides a live DOM panel behind a scene-depth-tested canvas aperture on WebGPU, full compatibility and alpha-enabled preview. packages/html/test/blend-browser.js proves zero RGBA on uncovered panel pixels, intact opaque foreground and off-panel scene pixels, moving/resizing/hidden/removed panels, live input and disposal. A rectangular mask matches the requested blending row; transparent CSS inside that rectangle reveals the page beneath it, rather than reconstructing scene content behind the panel. It does not claim DOM pointer occlusion by scene geometry.





## Batch 262 texture-viewer row

Optional inspector/textures provides a startup capture and a late-attached viewer of
all captured live GPUTextures, including metadata and mip/channel/layer previews.
This implements the native texture exploration row described in the cited public
inspector descriptions on native WebGPU and the WebGL2 compatibility layer. Texture
painting is outside this row. It cannot enumerate
unknown resources made before capture, an explicit WebGPU restriction. The browser
fixture creates every source before opening the panel and checks exact mip/channel,
array-layer/3D-slice/1D, uint display range, sRGB, depth/stencil and multisample pixels,
plus the existing CC0 Poly Haven real-image data. CPU tests cover descriptor integrity,
resource lifecycle, cancellation, weak bookkeeping and wrapper composition.
Compatibility uses the same WebGPU-facing API. No raw numeric export or speed parity
is claimed; restrictions are in limits.md.

Native and compatibility assertions pass all listed formats/subresources,
including stencil and restoration of depth sampling on the same texture.
The layered-capture regression also proves preview outputs stay out of every
capture from this module, including list change notifications.

Unknown indirect/bundle work remains explicit. No claim about shader invocations, unique geometry or elapsed speed.

The unchanged canonical native/compatibility shadow-cull driver passes exact

html/inline embeds same-origin/CORS images, computed CSS image URLs and matching document/adopted/imported font faces into data URLs before foreignObject drawing. packages/html/test/inline-browser.js validates 1,893 real image pixels and exact 92 px live/embedded font width, using a separate CORS server and existing licensed assets. packages/html/test/inline.test.js covers URL resolution, deduplication, form-state regression, import cycles, error rejection and abort/deadline semantics. ### Batch 273 Firefox compatibility evidence
Shipped Firefox159.0.1 on Windows executes all46 feature/compute cases against native Chromium reference data with original tolerances, all733 recorded render pipelines and477 compute dispatches. ## Batch 237: cubic overlapping synthesis
Every returned N-cube window is learned from the voxel sample, including all-axis periodic boundaries. Tests check N=2 and N=3 output-window membership, all 256 fully pinned binary cubes, pin preservation, copied samples, invalid inputs, contradictory odd periodic volumes, rotation/reflection cardinalities and deterministic seed replay. Native WebGPU and the WebGL2 compatibility browser routes solve the same pinned 4-cube checkerboard while rendering. No speed, global playability or unbiased distribution claim.
