- Batch 220 job 1: math/compat supplies all 17 requested porting methods on seven familiar classes, with original per-type bundles unchanged. Proof: packages/math/test/methods.test.js and existing math.test.js small-import assertions. Six Euler orders, native/separate camera round trips on both depth conventions, ray/segment constraints, closed triangle/plane contacts, degenerate inputs and explicit color spaces are covered. This is mathematical correctness evidence, not a timing claim.
# Batch 204 optional optimization

Exporters `/sparse` writes sparse accessors and interleaved vertex streams while
retaining every decoded value and accessor index/type/bound. The regression suite
checks morph byte reduction, matrix padding, integer normalization and all three
sparse index widths. [Sources and limits](../exporter-accessor-layout.md).

Exporters `/splats` writes ratified KHR Gaussian splat GLBs and raw .splat files
from SplatData. Full SH, covariance, linear color and opacity round-trip through
the independent gltf-pro reader; the existing raw codec is reused. Real-capture
evidence and precision boundaries: [splat record](../exporter-splats.md).

The optional exporters `/materials` entry writes data-scene KHR material payloads,
texture maps and primitive-local variants, including shared default meshes with
different alternatives and alternate-only image resources. Regression and
Khronos validator evidence: `packages/exporters/tests/materials-entry.test.js`.
Sources and boundaries: [material record](../exporter-materials.md).

`@margoengine/exporters/optimize-extra` adds independently controlled redundant animation-key reduction, unused resource pruning and static sibling GPU instancing. The regression suite checks linear and STEP behavior, quaternion signs, morph-weight layout, resource reachability, exact instance TRS, multi-scene protection, opaque extension retention, cancellation and input immutability. The existing Khronos Box fixture supplies real licensed geometry; output validator errors remain zero. This is behavior evidence, with no measured speed claim or assertion that every asset shrinks.


## Batch 237: WFC assembly
assembleWFC converts solved IDs and source/orientation metadata into shared-geometry tile meshes from loaded glTF models. CPU tests cover every proper cube rotation, reflection, nested transforms, ownership, invalid inputs and rollback. assemble-browser.mjs checks four placed instances in one draw on native WebGPU and the WebGL2 compatibility layer. This is deterministic instancing evidence, with no speed claim.






Batch 249 job 2: ecs/relation-query matches exact and wildcard relationship pairs with component filters and exposes target entity ids and component rows. Proof: packages/ecs/test/relation-query.test.js; no performance claim.


Batch 267 job 2: asset tools search all three official catalogues, reject non-CC0 Khronos legal chains, download complete relative resources with licence/hash manifests and install real models, HDRIs and textures through existing optional packages. CPU: packages/mcp/tests/assets.test.js; reproducible real downloads: packages/mcp/tests/assets-prepare.mjs; both-backend rendered loading: packages/mcp/tests/assets-browser.mjs. This does not claim every variant is browser-decodable or compare speed.

## Batch 237: hexagonal WFC
Six-neighbor hex layouts and eight-neighbor prisms preserve reciprocal topology on all five coordinate layouts, including even-parity periodic seams and mask holes. CPU tests compare tiny graphs against exhaustive assignments, verify all side transforms geometrically, and check 19-cell masked hex boards, copy ownership, seeded replay, pins and contradiction/abort behavior. Both rendering routes also solve and validate every side and vertical edge of a periodic odd-r 6x6x2 prism.

### Batch273 scenery scene capture
All original scenery correctness checks pass on native WebGPU and compatibility: four displayed presets, HDR fog transmittance, switching/disposal, stars and aurora, at1x and4x. A test-only compatibility queue barrier replaces a premature canvas read; native capture remains in the submit task. No runtime rendering, tolerances, sample counts or work grids change. Regression: packages/scenery/test/presented-frame.test.js.

### Batch 239 job 5: EXT_structural_metadata property reads
Claim: optional tiles/properties reads primitive property attributes and texture data, applying schema transforms while retaining raw integer/channel semantics. Evidence: packages/tiles/tests/properties.test.js checks a CC0 6876-point cloud exhaustively, a real transparent property PNG against golden values, custom byte oracles, sparse accessors, matrix padding, codec precision guards, malformed resources and cancellation. packages/tiles/tests/tiles-browser.ts renders the classified cloud and verifies raw texture samples through both GPU backends. Functional parity is compared to the documented 3d-tiles-renderer StructuralMetadata vertex and triangle/barycentric query surface, cited in docs/clean-room.md; no speed claim. Source triangle indices are required; optimized/deformed pick remapping is outside this query entry. Limits and raw-codec requirements are explicit in docs/limits.md.

Batch 225 job 3: signed point offsets maintain normal distance, cubic offsets verify interval error bounds between samples, and reversal preserves all existing cubic geometry. Proof: packages/splines/test/offset.test.js checks independent distance/perpendicularity, dense source-mapped approximation errors within reported bounds, alternate normals and negative distances, seams, bevel ranges, reversal across all kinds, singular inputs and explicit bounded-work failure. The bound excludes bevel connectors; no topology cleanup, exact directed-rounding certification or speed claim.

### Multi-worker BVH with serial tree parity (batch 221)

bvh/parallel distributes one mesh's SAH subtrees across a bounded pool, with planning/packing off the page thread and a shared-position or cloned-input path. packages/bvh/tests/parallel.test.js compares every packed word, index and original face id with MeshBVH.build, including one million triangles over four real leaf workers, SAH/center strategies, global depth caps, coincident centroids, float64 and interleaved vertices. It also compares queries/refits/restores and checks abort, deadline and worker failure cleanup. The production Vite consumer checks serial parity and emits both nested module-worker assets; bvh's existing browser suite runs the unchanged WebGPU/WebGL2 query and compute coverage. This proves correctness and worker distribution, not a wall-clock speedup or whole-package superiority. packages/bvh/tests/index-order.test.js proves the existing index-preserving default already covers batch221 job 2's indirect requirement, without allocating or assigning an index buffer on the caller.

Batch 249 job 4: ecs/serialize provides binary world snapshots and baseline-checked deltas of selected components, independent of save. Proof: packages/ecs/test/serialize.test.js. A sparse change fixture transfers fewer bytes than its full incompressible snapshot and reconstructs identical bytes; no timing or compression-optimality claim.

Batch 267 job 3: optional mcp/http serves unchanged MCP tools, resources, prompts and image content over JSON reply mode of current 2026-07-28 and legacy 2025 Streamable HTTP. Current metadata, discovery, encoded/name/parameter header validation, stateless request lifecycle, result envelopes and disconnect cancellation are proved by packages/mcp/tests/http-modern.test.js. Real HTTP proof: packages/mcp/tests/http.test.js covers negotiation, media/framing/status codes, CORS/Host/auth/version checks, principal/session isolation, repeated duplicate ids, explicit versus disconnect cancellation, deadlines, capacity, idle expiry with a controlled clock, late factory disposal, response bounds and split/malformed UTF-8. Legacy standalone SSE is explicitly declined and current GET/DELETE returns 405; no subscription capability is advertised. No remote identity-provider or performance claim.
Batch 208 job 1: cache/stream delivers a cold full-file response before the server ends its body and persists only complete bytes. Evidence: packages/cache/test/stream.test.js (no read-ahead, cancellation, AbortSignal, failure, over-budget pass-through) and packages/cache/scripts/test-browser.js (server holds the SheenChair tail until the browser receives the 256-byte prefix, then verifies SHA-256 equality of delivered and stored bodies). Matches Workbox's streaming-response behavior for this row, adding the existing IndexedDB budget and eviction controls; no timing or throughput claim.
## Batch 172 job 1: demand-driven scientific pipeline

Claim: optional sciviz/pipeline reuses unchanged ancestors and executes only downstream nodes after a parameter change; temporal dependency is inherited and independent branches retain their times. ### Animation plugin registration and cache-load lifetime

Animation controllers detach their registered identity, preserving same-name
plugins in another scene. GeometryCachePlayer keeps its loading scene through
the initial asynchronous read and releases its external abort subscription on
failure or disposal. Disposed crowd, LOD, morph and interactivity controllers
reject reattachment. Proof: `packages/animation/tests/scene-owner.test.js`,
`disposed-owner.test.js` and `tests/animation-owner-contract.test.js`; the
fixtures use the real engine registry with a recording GPU. These repairs do
not claim a complete engine-wide lifetime audit or any speed improvement.

- Batch 220 job 2: math/curve supplies linear, held-step, smoothstep and nonuniform cubic scalar interpolation, editable keyed curves, channel sets and packed sample interpolants. packages/math/test/curve.test.js proves analytic values, random seeking, exact keys, endpoint clamps/derivatives, nonlinear nonuniform first-derivative joins, independent copies, edits, clamped sample tables and invalid inputs. The existing small-import tests remain unchanged. No monotonic-cubic, looping-animation, zero-allocation or elapsed-speed claim.

- Batch 220 inspector boundary repair: optional GL inspection uses the native context without loading the compatibility layer. Unchanged isolation.test.js passes; inspector gl.test.js proves registry-independent capture and resource/state cleanup after shader compilation/link failures. Existing GL browser checks retain exact restored pixels and captured draw/shader assertions.

React callbacks run by ascending finite priority, with stable ties. A positive subscriber owns rendering until removed, including demand and immersive XR frames. Optional exporters/textures supplies clean-room box, bilinear and Lanczos resizing with exact sRGB transfer and associated colour filtering, plus native WebP/AVIF output with MIME feature detection and coded unsupported-format errors. GLB output writes extension image sources with optional resized PNG/JPEG core fallbacks. Native WebP decoded correctly; this Chromium runtime did not support AVIF encoding, so its unsupported error was checked. The optional keyboard edits TextInput and TextArea through pointer, gamepad and world-panel rays, including selections, grapheme deletion and shift/caps modes. Evidence: keyboard.test.js and keyboard-browser.mjs; no typing-speed or headset hardware claim.





### Review 99 mesh builder checkpoint

Exactly zero-area Float32 triangles are counted and omitted; thin nonzero input
geometry is retained. Original triplet IDs remain stable after compaction and
mesh statistics update atomically in standalone and composed tracers. Proof:
mesh-build-regression.test.js fails twice on the prior Wasm, then passes; its
512-case independent scaled-integer oracle and five Rust tests pass. The 16-bin
SAH fixture reduces a gap ray's node checks from five to three. This proves
that fixture's work only, with no throughput, universal watertightness or
full-scene transport claim. The continuation passes the full mesh suite on native and WebGL2/Vulkan and the exact README example. The additional composed native run times out at its unchanged 12-minute lease; the separate compatibility run passes.

### Review 99 mesh checkpoint validation update

Mesh native and WebGL2/Vulkan transport, degeneracy statistics, original AOV IDs
and lifecycle pass, and the triangle-mesh README example runs as written. The
full composed native suite times out at the unchanged twelve-minute lease,
with subset 31 awaiting its trace pipeline. No full-scene or composed-native
acceptance claim is made; the failure is retained in known-bugs and the
checkpoint receipt. No timing result here is a throughput claim.

### Review 99 local vertex attributes

Indexed Float32 normals and UVs are snapshotted and gathered for nondegenerate
mesh faces. Independent analytic per-pixel sun/texture oracles pass in standalone
and composed tracers on native WebGPU and WebGL2/Vulkan at the existing mesh
1e-5 value bound; maximum observed error is below 4.76838e-7. The two new CPU
regressions fail against the preceding build, then the full package passes
147/147; six Rust cases pass. This is a local attribute checkpoint, not whole-
scene closure, a conservation guarantee for shading normals, or a speed claim.

### Review 99 composition timeout phase evidence

The retained failing native log proves lease exhaustion during pipeline readiness,
with subset 31's trace pipeline outstanding after preview/ids completion. It
does not prove a permanently stuck compile or identify a Dawn/driver subphase.
The new independent host watchdog reports pending operations and stops its child
tree within the unchanged lease. Deterministic regressions cover the recorded
event ordering, blocked phases and owned-tree termination. This repairs missing
diagnostics only; native acceptance and the earlier unattributed WebGL2 timeout
remain open. [Evidence](results/review-99-compose-timeout-20261109.json).

The changed supervisor's full WebGL2/Vulkan suite passes all original checks.
The required full-package README gate separately fails the resampled module's
20-second deadline. No retry was made and no checkpoint push is claimed.

### Review 99 shared-instance transport evidence

Explicit meshInstances share one object-space BLAS per geometry id in standalone
and composed tracers. Matrix-only updates reuse byte-identical BLAS; an exact
content change invalidates all referencing placements. Independent baked mesh
reference checks cover affine translation, rotation/nonuniform scaling, shear,
reflection, smooth normals and UVs, overlapping TLAS leaves, depth and stable
triangle ids. Native and WebGL2/Vulkan maximum color/depth errors are below 1e-5;
failed transforms preserve the committed image. CPU tests verify per-tracer cache
isolation, snapshots, restoration, disposal, invalidation and SIMD lane/tail
comparison. See results/review-99-shared-instances-20261109.json and the package
mesh-instances tests. This closes only the shared-instance implementation gap;
triangle NEE/RIS, independent guiding, many-light lobes/scattering, RIS naming
and equal-cost work, and root emitter-MIS suite coverage remain open. Full-package
README and native full-composition acceptance remain blocked in lane-U.

Evidence: whitepaper/results/batch243-mesh-pbr-20261109.json. No speed number is claimed.
### Batch 233 job 1: designed road intersections
Optional intersections unites rounded/mitered road footprints, applies explicit circular curb returns and generates separate raised curb and sidewalk layers on shared sloping ground. CPU proof: road/test/intersections.test.js, closed-form area/radius/plane equations. GPU proof: road/test/browser-check.mjs, independent layer pixels, reboot and disposal on both backends. Details and limits: [road-design.md](../road-design.md). No speed or certified civil-design claim.

### Batch 223: software occlusion evidence

The optional culling/webgl2 entry culls core batches on the full WebGL2 layer and
native WebGPU using current-frame conservative CPU depth. Its licensed Cesium Box
fixture draws 28 of 81 instances behind the wall, 81 when the wall moves away,
and 28 when it returns, with byte-identical pixels to the unculled scene on both
backends. Camera motion and a camera inside the crowd also match. This establishes
opaque mesh occlusion comparable to the documented Babylon WebGL2 query behaviour,
with conservative full-cell coverage instead of asynchronous queries. It does not
claim universal hidden-object rejection or a speed advantage. See clean-room.md.
### FBX texture formats (batch 205)

The optional textures entry preserves TGA, DDS, TIFF and BMP material images through the existing
GLB PBR path. Proof: `packages/fbx/test/textures.test.js` compares exact decoded PNG pixels for
embedded and fetched versions of all four formats, including BC1 DDS; tests hook composition in
both orders and rejection of recognized damaged input. `packages/decoders/tests/bmp.test.js` checks
row direction/padding, indexed pixels, RGB/alpha masks, OS/2, RLE escapes and invalid bounds.
This proves the requested format routing; it makes no claim of arbitrary vendor extensions.





### Batch 243 clean-room learned denoising
DenoiseML evaluates a clean-room trained 321-parameter albedo/normal-guided kernel-prediction network in WebGPU compute. No downloaded weights or external inference runtime are required. Final independent synthetic validation normalized MSE is 0.0516443066 versus raw 0.0557763427. Native GPU synthetic-plane MSE is 0.0656393536 versus raw 0.0836865911; the independently rendered licensed Box holdout is 0.0001771820 versus raw 0.0002043207 against a 512-spp reference. Compatibility uses the same raw image/AOVs and reaches 0.0001771820. Maximum GPU/CPU output difference is 3.5762786865234375e-7 (synthetic) and 5.960464477539063e-8 (Box), below unchanged 1e-4. Constant HDR/coverage and real replacement-device reconstruction pass on both backends, with no validation or shader errors. These examples establish the feature and measured improvements for these inputs, not blanket superiority over other denoisers.
Receipt: results/batch243-denoise-ml-20261109.json; package tests/fixtures/denoise-ml-training.json records the training parameters and Float32 weight SHA-256.


### Batch 209: logic-only scene stage
headless/null provides actual engine meshes, scene isolation, transform hierarchy, animation sampling and plugin ticks without graphics. packages/headless/tests/null.test.js runs with a navigator.gpu getter that throws, verifies child world matrices and a second scene, checks actual animation property/skeleton sampling, and asserts render passes never execute. This establishes the scene/animation/plugin scope of the NullEngine parity row; GPU deformation is not simulated.
### Batch 172 job 3: clip and threshold
The optional sciviz/clip entry cuts supported unstructured/image cells while interpolating every point tuple and retaining every cell tuple. clip.test.js proves all tetra sign cases and complementary volumes, exact interior boxes and disjoint complements, scalar iso-volumes, surface areas, structured strides, curvilinear coordinates, threshold modes, provenance and cancellation. clip-browser.mjs renders the attributed TCIA CT-ORG threshold and NASA cut on both backends. Sphere clipping is the documented sampled-implicit approximation; no analytic sphere or validated anatomical segmentation claim. No speed result.
Batch 206: optional USD PointInstancer expansion shares prototype geometry, preserves prototype-local SRT and stable-ID masks, and supports a selected sampled pose. Proof: packages/usdz/tests/instancer.test.js against OpenUSD 26.11 generated matrices; scripts/modern-usdz-tests.js checks actual model rendering. No speed claim or claim of vectorized-array animation playback.

### Selection glow/fill (batch 199 job 4)
The optional highlight entry adds independently styled translucent surface fill and inner/outer soft glow from visible selected pixels. Two half-float separable passes preserve small halo values; mask-only rendering performs no JFA compute or distance-field allocation. Proof: packages/selection/test/highlight.test.js and highlight.browser.mjs on native and full compatibility, with fill, inner/outer separation, radius support, full-occlusion equality, resource reuse and restore/disposal checks. No speed number is claimed.

### Mixed geospatial vectors (batch 202)
All five requested format families produce point/line/polygon primitives with inspectable feature attributes. `packages/loaders/tests/geo.test.js` proves holes by exact area, multipart and mixed feature ownership, original double coordinates, Shapefile Z/M/dBASE alignment, GPX segment boundaries, KML nested placemarks, MVT 64-bit IDs/commands and hostile binary rejection. It also reads all 127 Natural Earth land features. The browser fixture renders a holed parcel through the existing mesh helper on each backend. No speed or map-style-renderer parity claim is made.
### Batch 233 job 2: spline lane paint
Optional road/lanes builds lane-border/edge lines and arrows in arc-distance/lateral coordinates on curves and grades, with clipped junction zones and phase-continuous connector splines. Proof: road/test/lanes.test.js (analytic circular offsets, straight boundary positions, phase, slope, closed seam, arrow turns and validation), road/test/browser-check.mjs (curved paint pixels, exact reboot and disposal, both backends). No speed claim. [Limits and sources](../road-design.md).
### Browser UI layout authoring (batch 259)
The optional editor provides a live engine preview, element tree/palette, editable properties, source/style editors, drag/drop hierarchy edits, bounded undo/redo and actual JSON/XML/CSS save/export plus project reopen. Validation is atomic and invalid drafts cannot silently save stale source. Evidence: editor.test.js and editor-browser.mjs on native WebGPU and compatibility WebGL2, including 320px viewport checks.

## Batch 210 job 3: boolean and string tracks
animation/discrete-tracks provides typed hold-left tracks, live property binding and categorical action clips. A binary-search sampler is checked against an independent key scan, and mixer tests verify original-weight influence, winner/tie behavior and restoration without numeric coercion. Native WebGPU and WebGL2 compatibility render the same five Fox visibility/text states, including zero hidden draws and original restoration. The categorical entry is usable without importing a rendering runtime; mixer integration remains optional. No timing or full-engine equivalence result is claimed.
Batch 239 job 4 claim: tile replacement uses the same complementary ordered screen-door ranks for meshes, point discs and globally sorted Gaussian content; reversal and nested refinement keep a partition along each hierarchy path. Proof: packages/tiles/tests/fade.test.js, packages/tiles/tests/splats.test.js, packages/points/tests/dither-fade.test.js, packages/splats/test/dither-fade.test.js and the fade case in packages/tiles/tests/tiles-browser.mjs on both backends. This is 16-rank spatial coverage, not a claim of temporal antialiasing or stochastic transparency quality.





### Batch 243 mesh emitter and photometric light proposals
The optional mesh entry samples triangle emitters and spot/Type C IES sources at scattering vertices. Independent CPU/WGSL probes establish world-area selection under nonuniform/reflected placements, quantized-CDF reciprocal MIS, inverse-square/range intensity and irregular angle interpolation. Mesh PBR proposals preserve per-sample emissive texture, mask and side tests. Pixel evidence is produced by packages/voxel-pathtracer/tests/mesh-lights-browser.mjs against an independent rectangular-area quadrature, point photometry, occlusion and decoded texture radiance. This is correctness evidence, not a speed claim or blanket cross-engine quality ranking.

Native evidence: results/batch243-mesh-lights-native-20261109.json, all six cases, maximum rectangular-integral RGB error 2.76440661716032e-5 against 2e-4; spot/IES maximum error 1.1650789509709547e-5 against 1e-4. That initial compatibility run was blocked at its unchanged 120 s render-image signal; the later canonical native/compatibility run passes all rows (results/batch243-mesh-lights-20261109.json). Historical timeout cause remains unisolated. All 166 package unit tests pass; the dependent CPU sweep had 873 passes, three stale umbrella failures subsequently repaired by generation/rebuild (all eight umbrella tests pass), and one pre-existing skip.

## Batch 207 job 2: lightmap bake and export
The Blender addon bakes diffuse direct/indirect illumination onto a second UV set, embeds linear normalized PNG plus HDR scale, and blender/lightmap reads and applies it through the new owning lightmap/imported API. Independent unit-sun irradiance checks cover every fixture texel; source UV0 differs from UV1. A second fixture proves UV1 generation. Native WebGPU and compatibility WebGL2 draw the analytic neutral-display colour (56,56,56) with zero validation errors, unchanged when engine direct/ambient lights rise to 100, and release mesh handles. Python verifies restoration after success and failure. This closes the offline second-UV lightmap delivery row of web engines; geometry instances and dynamic lighting remain as documented limits.
Batch 219 job 3: optional geometry/teapot tessellates the real 32-patch Utah Teapot data with selectable body/lid/bottom, fitted lid and historical proportions. Evidence: packages/geometry/tests/teapot.test.js independently evaluates every patch using de Casteljau, checks derivative normals against differences and pole limits, exact mesh counts at four resolutions, option independence, UVs, finite values and parameter validation. The geometry GPU suite uploads the actual model on both rendering backends, verifies outward winding and a displayed red teapot. This proves the named shape/controls, not solid topology, matching vertex order or speed.


### Batch 246 job 1: ordered map images
Optional tilemap/objects draws tile cells, tile objects and image layers together in painter order with exact texel loads. CPU expectations cover anchors, animation, ordering and transforms; real GPU readback covers collection images, repeated layers, all flip combinations, quarter-turn objects and empty maps on both backends. Evidence: packages/tilemap/test/objects.test.js and objects-check.mjs. No speed claim.


### Batch 209: GIF frame streams
headless/gif uses the exporter writer and exporters/palette quantizer without duplication. Existing capture tests retain exact palettes, transparency, median-cut error bars, LZW growth/reset coverage and cumulative timing. New stream tests independently decode repeat policy and variable delays, assert pending-source abort/closure, and regress long-run 40 fps rounding. Native WebGPU and full WebGL2 compatibility render three cube frames that Chromium ImageDecoder reads with zero pixel difference and 40,000 microsecond delays. This proves those fixtures and codec controls, not a universal perceptual-quality or speed ranking.

### Batch 224 hull and Minkowski capabilities
The optional mesh hull retains original double positions, including a unit cube at offset 1e9 that float32 cannot preserve. General Minkowski sums retain nonconvex topology: L-shape plus cube has volume 16, two L-shapes have volume 26, and a 3-unit hollow box plus a 0.2-unit cube has volume 3.2^3 - 0.8^3 = 32.256. Independent tests check closure, nondegenerate faces, source ownership, convex-pair equivalence and translation covariance. Exact BSP classification precedes floating cell export and hulls; no exact-output, minimal-cell or speed claim is made. See clean-room references and numeric/work limits.


### Batch 265 texture integration

The optional React textures entry uses native image, KTX2 and video Texture values on native Material classes. Normal, roughness and emissive map inputs preserve Scene ownership and core remains unchanged. Evidence: packages/react/tests/textures.test.js, packages/materials/tests/shared-pass.test.js and packages/react/scripts/test-textures-browser.mjs. Reproduction and exact supported inputs are in docs/limits.md and reproduce.md; this is a functionality claim, not a speed result.
Batch 206 implicit shapes: optional USD Cube, Sphere, Cylinder, Capsule and Cone conversion preserves dimensions, cardinal axes, hierarchy and materials. Proof: shapes.test.js against 22 OpenUSD 26.11 extent results, normal/winding checks and modern-usdz-tests.js actual rendering. ### Configurable large FBX imports (batch 205)

The large entry imports a 200 MiB valid ASCII file and a separate binary mesh with 2,500,002
distinct expanded vertices. `packages/fbx/test/large.test.js` proves the first has identical GLB
output to its unpadded source, and the second retains every position and index. The 200 MiB case
uses a long legal comment to isolate the input-byte cap, not a claim about a 200 MiB mesh workload.
Each configurable parse/scene/geometry/output cap is independently exercised at a rejecting value.
GPU tests also prove custom vertex caps survive worker serialization and successful loads render
through both backends. These are correctness claims without wall-clock or memory-usage claims.

The large binary mesh is also attached as a real PBRModel on a CPU-only engine and through the actual parser worker. Both retain the configured 512 MiB byte cap, with decoded GLB buffers above 64 MiB; no GPU upload is mocked into the rendering proof. The separate browser proof uses the real GPU.

### Batch 233 job 3: road network input
GeoJSON lines and OSM XML/Overpass ways produce source-tagged local metre road networks, lane/width metadata, direction and grade separation. GeoJSON intersections split; OSM node identity governs connectivity. Proof: road/test/geojson.test.js with analytic WGS84 equations, topology/metadata/schema checks and real attributed Times Square ways; road/test/browser-check.mjs renders a real imported road on both backends with exact reboot and disposal. No complete GIS/routing-restriction or speed claim. [Limits and sources](../road-design.md).

### Batch 172 job 4: probes and field integrals
The optional probe entry supplies a reusable locator, barycentric and true trilinear sampling, linked polyline profiles, exact cell-model integrals and image/dataset resampling. probe.test.js checks affine and distorted-hexa sampling, source tuples/IDs, signed images, line/area/volume integrals, a varying-Jacobian trilinear oracle, masks, ownership, empty input and pipeline cancellation. probe-browser.mjs exercises real NASA and OpenNeuro data, chart pointer events and 3D markers, coverage, exact dataset/profile agreement and disposal on both GPU backends. No exact-predicate, conservative-remapping or speed claim.

Batch 223 oriented boxes: the three optional /bounds subclasses preserve the
unculled images while rejecting long thin meshes, model primitives and dynamic
instances whose spheres overlap the view. Native Hi-Z also rejects rods behind
an opaque wall using their box near depth. The box support test is checked
against transformed corners for reflection and shear; model bounds include
caller-supplied displacement padding. WebGL2 model Hi-Z remains outside this
claim, as documented in limits.md. No performance result is claimed.

With exact coplanar patch coverage, the same job 1 scene now keeps 1 of 81 rows behind the wall on both backends, still with byte-identical pixels; moving the wall restores 81 immediately. This is a deterministic visibility count, not a speed claim.


## Batch 207 job 3: lossless .margo meshopt
The optional meshopt entry compresses geometry with Margo's existing encoder and reads it with its existing decoder. Static, compact, uint16, animated, object hierarchy, curves and lightmap fixtures preserve every section byte and scene value. Download bytes are never increased; the animated fixture saves 9408 of 28924 bytes. Corrupt streams and decoded-size bombs reject before GPU upload. Original/compressed static and animated rendering is byte-identical across both GPU backends, including animation deformation, with no validation errors and released mesh handles. No throughput, quantization or comparison-engine timing claim.

### Batch 246 job 2: Tiled import parity row
Templates, image layers, text objects and Wang sets are imported rather than warned away. Tests cover XML/JSON, template-only and existing tileset remapping, instance overrides, relative resources, cached loads, pending abort, cycles, sparse images, style defaults and exact eight-slot mixed Wang matching. Imported image/template output has exact GPU readback on native WebGPU and WebGL2 compatibility. Proof: packages/tilemap/test/tiled-full.test.js, tiled.test.js and objects-check.mjs. No speed claim or claim that fromTMX can restore data its input omitted.
### Batch 211 job 1: state machines per layer

animator/layers composes independently switching graph layers over locomotion, using masks and fractional override/additive weights. Proof: packages/animator/test/layers.test.js (transition, clocks, ordering, late install, cleanup) and test/layers-browser.mjs (real Fox skin rendering). No speed or visual-quality superiority claim. Public layer behaviour compared against PlayCanvas Layers and Masks; research record in docs/clean-room.md.

Optional structures builds closed bridge slabs, terrain-founded supports and arch/box tunnel shells with open annular portals, following grades and authored/terrain-derived spans. It rejects insufficient crossing clearance and excludes piers from other road footprints. No terrain-solid excavation, certified structural capacity or speed claim. [Full limits and research](../road-design.md).

### Batch 261 GPU label correctness
The optional GPU label entry batches 100,000 single-glyph labels into one instanced draw. WebGPU, native WebGL2 and compatibility WebGL2 render a 32 px Margo control at 87 by 30 raster pixels from both 5 and 50 world units; a coincident 100k collection retains one label with 99,999 exact comparisons. This is deterministic correctness/work evidence, not a throughput claim or complete rival API match. Reproduce with packages/labels/scripts/test-browser.js; see docs/labels-gpu.md.

API-index aggregate consistency (batch 206): the normal root test suite checks that every package row, including margoengine, contributes to the summary counts; full api:check compares row contents with compiled declarations. Proof: tests/api-report-scope.test.js and scripts/api-report.js. No API compatibility guarantee is inferred from aggregate counts.



### Scenery textured lunar disc

`@margoengine/scenery/moon` draws a sampled sRGB albedo sphere at infinity with
sunlit terminator, fixed mean-Earth orientation, independent lunar north and
complementary-phase earthshine. No core/stage change. CPU:
`packages/scenery/test/moon.test.js`; GPU: `packages/scenery/test/moon-gpu.mjs`
with NASA's public-use CGI Moon Kit map and synthetic orientation landmarks.

no measured astronomical photometric accuracy is claimed.

### Cluster LOD (batch 222)
`@margoengine/lod/clusters` builds a boundary-preserving spatial meshlet tree and renders independently chosen regions using projected error and complementary pixel intervals. CPU proof: packages/lod/test/clusters.test.js (triangle conservation, meshlet limits, mixed-depth boundary edges, error scaling, complete dither paths, cancellation); mesh-edit/tests/simplify.test.js (pinned vertices and accumulated displacement). Browser proof: packages/lod/test/clusters-check.mjs on the attributed FlightHelmet geometry. This claims adaptive cluster rendering, not GPU-driven Nanite performance or virtual geometry streaming.
Cluster LOD GPU proof (batch 222): on the existing CC0 FlightHelmet primitive, both backends select 5686 source triangles near and 4734 rendered triangles at a 16 pixel target, with 2068 of 2102 source lit pixels covered. The browser also runs LODFade and two cluster renderers together; no GPU validation errors. This is a deterministic draw-work/image result, with no wall-clock claim. Broader GPU-driven batching remains a quality optimization, not part of the claimed CPU selection path.



### Cinematic physical lens (batch 216 job 1)
The optional lens uses focal length and fitted sensor size in millimetres for vertical FOV and sends f-number, focus and sensor fit to existing DOF. Proof: cinematic/test/physical-lens.test.js covers 192 sensor/focal/aspect/fit combinations, focus tracking and atomic validation. physical-lens-browser.mjs renders the same diagnostic card at f/1 and f/16 on native WebGPU and WebGL2 compatibility, checking changed blur width, physical focal length and unchanged FOV. No speed claim or compound-lens simulation claim.



## Batch 207 job 4: live node graphs
The optional Blender nodes entry keeps common Mix, Color Ramp, Noise and Math graphs procedural and socket-animatable. The own CC0 fixture contains all four families, no image sections and an animated Value socket sampled at three frames. A separate Cycles emission bake supplies independent RGB oracles for the connected Math -> Ramp -> Mix chain. GPU checks compare the displayed three frames and runtime override/rewind on native and compatibility backends, numerically exercise all 41 scalar operations, and render every listed blend, ramp color/interpolation and noise dimension/fractal variant. Noise W changes the image and restoring it restores identical pixels. These tests prove live behavior and bounded numerical agreement on that chain; they do not claim exact stochastic noise correspondence or full material-system parity. Surface resources belong to the existing PBRModel; disposal is checked after rendering.

### Batch 261 crossing-free angled leaders
Balanced planar matching assigns projected anchors to separated column ports, then extends them horizontally to reordered label boxes. Independent exact binary-rational intersection checks support the crossing-free claim for fractional and extreme-scale inputs; seeded integer checks also reject touches and overlapping segments. Coincident anchors share their origin only. The layout produces nonoverlapping boxes and zero SVG geometry writes on an unchanged frame. The geometric construction and limits are in docs/labels-callouts-route.md; no optimum-length or speed claim.

### Immediate debug and endpoint arcs (batch 227 job 3)
Immediate lines batch by style and expire on the next rendered frame while all views share the current frame; picking preparation preserves queued commands. Persistent arcs expose ordinary Line picking and materials. Planar and spherical arc sampling keeps exact endpoints and finite coincident/antipodal cases. Proof: packages/lines/tests/debug.test.js and debugLines in packages/lines/tests/features-browser.js, run on both backends at 1x/4x MSAA. Tests prove draw counts, not timing; speed measurements remain deferred.

### Batch 238 capture formats
The optional formats entry reads KSplat 0.1 levels 0/1/2 and SuperSplat compressed PLY, and writes standard Gaussian PLY in binary little-endian or ASCII. packages/splats/test/formats.test.js compares the real MIT raccoon capture against 64 black-box getter samples per KSplat level, checks all compressed-PLY word fields and SH degrees 0-3 against independent attributes, rejects malformed layouts and round-trips the real capture's positions exactly and colour/SH/covariance within float32 conversion bounds. The GPU formats-check renders the three KSplat derivatives and PLY export on each backend. Covariance normalization/floor and the display/linear SH approximation are retained accepted differences. No runtime or compression-quality claim.


### Batch 218 job 2: transition preset library

Eight clean-room presets support validated direction, edge softness, easing and treatment-specific parameters for frozen and live inputs. Proof: presets.test.js checks validation and owned-resource lifecycle; presets-browser.js independently computes gradient pixel expectations for all eight treatments, cardinal directions and 1x/4x. This is numerical correctness coverage, not a speed or physical paper simulation claim.

### Batch 229 job 2: ground-clamped vectors through the RVT
terrain/overlays supplies the requested metre-width polylines, filled polygons with holes and projected GeoJSON features with live updates. It paints the actual RVT and its steep/far fallback, and other materials can read the cached result. Cached imagery can sit below vectors. Evidence: overlays.test.js, overlays.browser.mjs, full terrain CPU/GPU suites and dependent CPU tests. The native fixture invalidates 20852 texels for first vector edits and 16548 for a width edit, against 262144 texels for all four whole levels; a still frame bakes zero. These are deterministic work counts without a timing or engine-speed claim. Limits and unclaimed options are in docs/terrain-overlays.md.
### Batch 247 job 2: GI correctness coverage
The GI entry now includes painted finite-order diffuse interreflection and temporal reuse guarded by exact input equality. Named analytic/painted visibility and energy tests apply; static frames reuse computed intervals and changed frames match forced-fresh output. Direct buffer observations prove reuse, while independently constructed analytic boundary faces prove painted bounce orders 0/1/2/4. Source: packages/lighting2d/tests/gi-history-regressions.js and sdf.test.js, alongside the unchanged gi-regressions suite. Native and full WebGL2 compatibility run the same assertions. No arbitrary-scene physical-exactness, converged solution, noise-reduction or timing claim.

### Orthographic cinematic cameras (batch 216 job 2)
The optional ortho entry supports follow through the existing spring rig, screen-space framing with dead/soft zones and lookahead, sphere group framing by full visible height, and live/interrupted orthographic height blends. CPU proof: cinematic/test/ortho.test.js checks independent projection geometry, depth independence, roll, portrait aspect, custom up, damping subdivision, membership/weight/size limits, cuts and mode endpoints. ortho-browser.mjs checks actual picks at authored screen points at two depths, group endpoints, rendered pixels and blend heights on native WebGPU and WebGL2. No claim about continuous mixed-projection blends or speed.
### Batch 266 effect helpers
Stars, Sparkles, Trail, Decal and Edges are available from the optional React effects-extra entry. Each uses native owning-package rendering and geometry. Proof: effects-extra.test.js plus test-effects-extra-browser.mjs in packages/react, with exact native pixel comparisons for all five helpers on WebGPU and compatibility WebGL2. Animated point attributes use the CPU and native partial update API; there is no GPU-only simulation or speed claim.

The optional mesh-pbr material and glTF snapshot path includes all four requested extension lobes and their factor/texture channels. Batch 201 real-asset comparison: the standard file's two independently authored bottles, rendered individually at the same transform, differ by mean 0.04697 (WebGPU) and 0.04622 (compatibility WebGL2) out of 255 per RGBA channel at 128x128, with a maximum edge/texel difference of 32. The fixture requires mean below one channel unit. This quantifies the documented conversion approximation; it is not a timing result. The three analytic endpoints remain bit exact.


- Batch 242 job 1: optional voxel-net/rooms provides isolated same-process worlds, lobby snapshots, join codes, atomic reserved capacity and single-use shard tickets. Proof: packages/voxel-net/tests/rooms.test.js; scope and defaults: docs/limits.md. No distributed matchmaking or speed claim.

### Batch 214 limit-aware swivel
Claim: clear swivel candidates that violate any supplied Euler, hinge or cone/twist limit are rejected inside the existing search, before acceptance and history commit. Evidence: packages/kinematics-constraints/tests/limits.test.js includes independent quaternion composition, equivalent Euler branches and gimbal lock, hinge-axis rejection, cone/twist rejection, NaN/missing rotations, copied limits, obstacle-plus-limit search, history preservation and hostile scratch mutation. limits-browser.mjs renders accepted legal poses on WebGPU and WebGL2 compatibility. The root entry imports no limits implementation.
### Batch 269: stereo and half-dome video
The optional video/dome material covers mono, side-by-side and top-bottom equirectangular video at 360 or 180 degrees, either eye. Native WebGPU and full WebGL2 compatibility pixel checks prove forward/left/right/up/down orientation, independent eye colours and black rear hemisphere for all twelve combinations on RTX A6000/Edge. No headset, other-browser or performance result is claimed. Ordinary video maps with repeat and optional mips were already available via materials.Texture; existing CPU tests prove upload progression, emissive binding, live wrapping and mip configuration. See docs/clean-room.md for normative and rival-contract comparison.

## Batch 173: categorical and registered volume fusion
Claim: optional sciviz/labels classifies integer categories, linear RGB/RGBA and independent scalar components in one ray march across independently registered source boxes. Evidence: packages/sciviz/tests/labels.test.js and labels-browser.mjs. Both WebGPU and WebGL2 compatibility give constant optical reference [109,0,109], exact equality between a two-component source and two scalar sources, zero image error for a 90-degree rotated varying field versus its aligned reference, and zero error after restoration. Exact adjacent uint32 IDs above 2^24 produce 2312 red and 2312 blue pixels with zero pixels of an absent green category. One-pixel outlines produce 2380 edge pixels, fewer than filled category pixels. Over gives [255,0,0], maximum [0,133,0]; disjoint depth intervals retain the same output after source order reversal. Real MSD MRI/segmentation and OpenNeuro RGBA demos are provided with separate dataset licences. This supports these defined behaviours only; no timing, clinical or whole-library parity claim.

The optional video/demux entry indexes AVC fragments and VP8/VP9/AV1 WebM and shares the existing exact timestamp seek/step and disposal implementation. ## Batch 173 job 3: cinematic volume lighting
The optional sciviz/cinematic entry provides independently enabled spherical local ambient occlusion, directional extinction shadows and progressive HG multiple scattering while sharing the existing volume classifier. Proof: cinematic.test.js and cinematic-browser.mjs; real EMD-8117 CC0 and TCIA CC BY 3.0 demo with source records. Both GPU backends give plain/shadow centre 82/73, exactly matching constant-field analytical references. At 256 samples the single-scattering image has central-patch mean 73.56 with RMSE about 5.03, down from 21.60 at 16 samples. A conservative white environment multiple-scattering reference gives 109.62/109.65 versus 111; g=0.6 reduces the backward-direction pixel to 33. Camera motion resets to sample 1; restoring the lighting resources changes zero pixel bytes. Work counters agree on both backends: 169400 scalar reads unlit, 4235000 with local AO, 5590200 with directional shadows for the 64x64 gradient-classified fixture. Those costs include classification gradients; the wrapper always writes its work attachment. No wall-clock speed or full-library parity claim. Limits and defaults: docs/limits.md; derivation and rival public-contract comparison: docs/clean-room.md.

RGBA32F packing preserves linear source texels, at an explicit memory/upload cost (limits.md). No speed claim or raised hardware limit.

### Batch 203 job 3: HDR6 correctness

The optional clean-room decoders/ktx2-hdr6 reads UASTC HDR 6x6i and native ASTC HDR 6x6 KTX2. The frozen independent fixture records prove byte-for-byte ASTC, half-float and RGB9E5 output for all 75 intermediate configurations in both stream versions, plus real HDR, noise, ramps and solids at every mip. Node worker tests cover concurrent jobs, transfer, cancellation and disposal. The browser GPU test uploads and reads back 393,216 half-float bytes per backend on WebGPU and WebGL2. Evidence: packages/decoders/tests/ktx2-hdr6.test.js, tests/hdr6-browser.mjs and native/hdr6/src/ise.rs. This is correctness evidence, not a throughput claim.

## Batch 177 job 1: vector 2D contours

Optional `/contour2d` constructs rectilinear marching-square isolines with asymptotic saddle disambiguation, adjacent filled polygon bands with holes, and scattered-point Delaunay contours. SVG retains vector paths and inline labels. Proof: `packages/sciviz/tests/contour2d.test.js` checks all 16 cases, saddle sign, affine sections, band area, masks, closed loops, thin/cocircular/edge-site triangulations, empty circumcircles, exact cancellation and input ownership. `contour2d-browser.mjs` checks both launch backends, both hash-pinned climate fields, both interpolation modes, nonempty rasterized SVG and vector/download structure. This claim concerns that explicit job checklist, not every option offered by general chart libraries; no measured speed claim. See limits.md for the interpolation and label-layout contract, and clean-room.md for papers and specification sources.


Batch 188 job 3: optional environment/reflector-pbr adds angle-dependent Fresnel and world-depth-dependent reflection blur. Native and full compatibility GPU HDR samples match independent CPU Schlick values with worst absolute error 0.00004981. A two-pixel stripe reflection has contact contrast 0.9375 native / 0.93210 compatibility, and zero contrast at full distance blur. Live IOR/distance updates compile no shader. Device replacement restores identical floor pixels; a real CC0 Venice Sunset panorama passes through the capture. Proof: packages/environment/tests/reflector-pbr.test.js and reflector-pbr-browser.js. Constant artist base term and box-mip approximation are explicit limits, not claims of full material or path-tracer parity.

## Precipitation cover (batch 236 job 2)
weather/cover stops rain, wetness, puddles, rings, snow cover and snow depth under roofs and trees from one top-down depth map, on core meshes and imported gltf-pro models, on WebGPU and WebGL2 compatibility. Proof: packages/weather/tests/cover.test.js and cover-check.html through parity-browser.mjs cover. 0 of 11,564 sheltered pixels change under full wetness and snow while 11,443 exposed pixels change; an out-of-view canopy probe proves the four-texel edge lookup. Shelter is limited to the camera-centred map (docs/limits.md). No speed claim.

## Batch 272 job 3: product floor caustics
staging/caustics traces arbitrary indexed glass/water surfaces with the existing public GPU BVH traversal, exact Snell/Fresnel transmission and integer photon deposits. It presents an additive floor independent of camera visibility. Proof: caustics.test.js and caustics-browser.mjs, analytic water/slab Fresnel, focusing, catcher pixels, cancellation, atomic replacement/recovery and actual device restore on both backends. Scope/exclusions: docs/staging-parity.md. No full-transport or speed claim.
### Batch 174 job 1: linked selections
The optional sciviz/link store resolves global, union and intersect view brushes by stable row keys, and propagates highlight, fade and filter membership to linked layer masks. Tests: packages/sciviz/tests/link.test.js checks the documented resolution truth tables, typed key identity, operations, atomic rejection and undo on 2MASS-derived predicates. packages/sciviz/tests/link-browser.mjs renders the real-data sky/histogram/heatmap on both backends, compares their changed pixels, and reads back a two-million-mark layer's mask. The stress layer repeats the existing million real observations; it is not a new two-million-row catalogue. One selection performs one 8,000,000-byte mask upload with geometry retained. This is a deterministic work-count claim, not a throughput claim. See docs/sciviz-link.md for scope and limits.

### Batch 186 job 2: one image, per-light screen demand
ShadowAtlas packs every logical shadow face into one depth image, selects point/spot resolution by viewport coverage and preserves directional/area shadow composition. CPU tests prove packing, memory/work counts and live camera-driven viewports; scripts/clustered-shadow-atlas-gpu.mjs checks blocked/clear production-shader probes for point/spot lights and combined rectangle/sphere shadows on both backends. The documented PlayCanvas shadow-atlas row is implemented. No speed, exact projected-sphere bound or incremental-update claim. Limits and accepted redraw trade-offs are in limits.md.

### Batch 52 job 4: cached atlas faces, update only what moved
ShadowAtlas re-renders only the faces whose light, tile or reaching casters changed, and keeps tiles in place while their size class holds. packages/clustered/test/shadow-atlas.test.js counts the redrawn faces (7 on the first frame, 0 when still, 1 for a box moved under a point light where every face redrew before, 1 for a hidden, shown, added or removed box, 1 for an added light) and checks that a partial redraw loads the atlas and clears only its tile. scripts/clustered-shadow-atlas-gpu.mjs renders a dark floor and tall box under low bright lights from close on a real GPU: the atlas at 512 texels a face matches LocalShadows at 512 (mean under 5% of the shadow darkening, worst at most 64 of 255), a still frame redraws no face and draws the identical picture, and a moved box redraws fewer than all faces. This is a work-count claim, not a speed claim.



## Batch 136: optional clean-room Draco encoding

Claim: compressDraco uses the original optional Rust/Wasm SIMD encoder without a core/stage dependency change. Sequential, standard/valence EdgeBreaker, position welding, independent corner-attribute seams and five prediction methods retain all oriented triangles and exact nonquantized attributes. Proof: exporters/draco-kernel unit tests and exporters/tests/draco-native.test.js compare the existing native decoder and opaque prebuilt decoder, including UINT32 boundaries, degenerate/nonmanifold inputs, shared seams, 3,000 deterministic normal directions and encode/free reuse. Existing draco.test.js protects GLB skins/weights/animations/images/bounds and the public entry contract. Browser exporter tests protect worker loading, cancellation and round-trip rendering on both backends. Entry gzip 181,081 -> 89,161 B; core plus stage remains 24,209 B. Eight output-bridge boundary calls are a deterministic bridge-work reduction, not a CPU speed claim. Sources and read scope: clean-room.md. Timing remains owed in the deferred speed phase.
Batch 191 job 6: post/curvature independently brightens convex normal gradients and darkens concave gradients, rejects depth discontinuities, and accepts owned or supplied world/view normals. Proof: curvature.test.js checks capture, restoration, validation and release; curvature-browser.mjs proves flat-field identity, correct gradient signs and world-normal orientation after a 90-degree camera change on both backends. This is view-dependent shape enhancement, not object-space curvature estimation.



## Batch 178 vector publication
The optional sciviz/export-vector entry writes depth-ordered paths for height fields, committed isosurfaces, grid triangle meshes, lines and point markers, resolving intersecting depth cycles by BSP splitting. CPU analytic oracles and native/compatibility real-data SVG/readback tests provide the proof; exact numerical/appearance limits and raster fallback are stated in ../sciviz-vector-export.md. Plotly's documented 3D SVG route embeds rasters (https://plotly.com/javascript/static-image-export/); this is a vector-output capability comparison, not a speed or GPU appearance-parity claim.

staging/backdrop provides a smooth real-mesh cyclorama plus original portrait/rembrandt/upfront/soft softbox rigs through the existing environment renderer. CPU proof: backdrop.test.js. ## Scientific dataset kinds, batch 176 job 1
Claim: a single optional normalized DataSet carries structured curvilinear and rectilinear grids, PolyData primitives, composites, polyhedral face streams and tessellated quadratic/Lagrange cells into existing batch 172 filters. Proof: packages/sciviz/tests/datasets.test.js and datasets-elements.test.js; all seven VTK quadratic families and six Lagrange families through order six have retained runtime output comparisons, with analytic checks through order ten. cutscene/scroll covers scroll scrubbing plus bounded repeated playback with alternate directions, including fractional/zero/infinite iteration counts. Batch 188 job 4: optional environment/exr-codecs decodes PXR24, B44/B44A, DWAA/DWAB and tiled files with all 133,200 RGBA components bit-identical to independent native OpenEXR 3.5.3 across twenty real photographic HALF/FLOAT scanline/tiled fixtures. ### Batch 201 job 3: draft audio emitter loading
The optional gltf-pro/audio entry delegates positional/global playback to the actual audio package classes, sharing decoded clips while preserving per-instance sources. The actual-device browser test loads both dialects, renders engine frames to update source poses, measures an offline equal-power PCM energy oracle and checks model disposal on native and compatibility backends. Sources are cited in clean-room.md. No speed claim.



Batch 136 proof: the 76-row four-setting real-model matrix in results/batch-136-draco-parity.json passes byte size, exact oriented corner preservation, independent native/prebuilt decoding, exact attribute equality and no-worse maximum/squared quantization error. Sparse high-precision RAW rANS has a regression preventing tagged-only fallback. Optional entry is now 91,248 B gzip versus the old 181,081 B; the correctness refinements add 2,087 B to the initial replacement. CPU proof covers shared ownership, disposal/cancellation and source/reconnect lifecycle. Browser proof in packages/stage/test/gpu/viewer-extras-check.mjs compares all RGBA pixels against direct viewer plus Animation on native WebGPU and webgl2-compat for four real asset classes, then checks actual picking, labels, variants, poster and custom-element replacement/reconnect. Desktop unsupported-AR feedback is tested; physical AR session presentation requires a compatible device. This is facade equivalence for the requested row, not universal model-viewer option parity or a speed result. See ../stage-viewer-extras.md and ../limits.md.
## Batch 177 job 5: live native traces

sciviz/stream attaches append and contiguous partial-channel patch methods to native line/scatter layers, with owned input snapshots, serialized commits, bounded rollover and AbortSignal. Figure.updateData retains native layer identity and style/visibility, building replacement GPU resources before freeing the old ones. WebSocket, fetch SSE and polling are async iterators with bounded messages, explicit failures and cancellation; SSE retains event metadata and polling follows consumer demand. SlidingTimeWindow supplies smooth original-unit x limits, including date axes and ChartPolish mappings. Evidence: stream.test.js covers rollover/order, copies, channels, concurrent updates, rejected patches, disposal/rebinding, in-flight fetch/parse/interval abort, byte-split SSE and HTTP 204, real local HTTP/WebSocket and source hashes. stream-browser.mjs uses frozen USGS events on WebGPU and compatibility WebGL2: 64 retained points, stable layer references/style, raw timestamps, patches/aborts, pick, combined controller hover, SVG geometry plus legend, pixel ink, resize and deterministic time interpolation. No allocation-free or speed claim.

### Batch 64 job 5: automatic scatter LOD rings
scatter/simplify builds Scatter's three LOD rings from one mesh by quadric-error edge collapse through lod's attribute-keeping chain over mesh-edit. Proof: packages/scatter/test/simplify.test.js (CPU, stand-in procedural meshes): a 2,208-triangle UV sphere gives rings of 883 and 265 triangles with deviation 0.017 and 0.063 of the radius; every ring vertex is a source vertex with its normal; a box keeps its hard normals and shape; the rings construct a Scatter; the closest-point bound is checked in every Voronoi region of a triangle. No GPU picture proof and no speed claim.

### Batch 67 job 1: water bodies from outlines and splines
water/bodies draws lakes, pools and rivers on one Water with their own levels, wave shares and colours, and nothing outside them. Evidence: packages/water/test/bodies.test.js checks exact signed distances, non-convex insides, spline levels and widths, overlap order and validation on the CPU; packages/water/test/bodies.browser.mjs checks on WebGPU that the dry probe shows the background, the river draws, the lake takes its colour, WaveQuery heights match the CPU body map (still lake below 1e-4 m, river below 2e-3 m), a still body has no wave velocity, and dispose restores the open-water frame exactly.

### List 3 batch 66 job 5: underwater view (2026-11-10)

Optional water/underwater adds Beer-Lambert fog and colour absorption, a near-plane waterline and single-scattered light shafts focused by the rendered surface when the camera is submerged, for Water and FFTOcean, and passes the scene through bit-exactly above water or when disabled. Proof: packages/water/test/underwater.browser.mjs (linear HDR recorders; extinction within 0.0007 relative of exp(-aL); exact rows above the independently projected waterline). Stand-in scene geometry; no timing claim.

Batch 234.3 completes underside transport: exact unpolarized dielectric Fresnel, Snell window and total internal reflection, screen-visible opaque reflection/refraction, RGB extinction on reflected water paths, borrowed sky and fallback medium. Native WebGPU and full WebGL2 on RTX A6000 prove 8,766 window pixels, 15,174 TIR pixels and 24,576 FFT pixels within 0.000489 linear RGB of the independent optical oracle (bar 0.002), matched IOR, environment and restoration, with single-sample and MSAA captures. The independent depth-plane suite proves all 1,026 rays with zero missed hits or false hits and distance error below 0.000006 m. [Recorded evidence](results/batch234-underwater-completion.json). Hidden/off-screen geometry is unavailable to screen traces; defaults, fallbacks and visit limits are in water-underwater.md. Original analytic fixtures, NOAA wind-driven preview; no timing or full-scene ray-tracing claim.
### Batch 67 job 1: water bodies from outlines and splines
water/bodies draws lakes, pools and rivers on one Water with their own levels, wave shares and colours, and nothing outside them. Evidence: packages/water/test/bodies.test.js checks exact signed distances, non-convex insides, spline levels and widths, overlap order and validation on the CPU; packages/water/test/bodies.browser.mjs checks on WebGPU that the dry probe shows the background, the river draws, the lake takes its colour, WaveQuery heights match the CPU body map (still lake below 1e-4 m, river below 2e-3 m), a still body has no wave velocity, and dispose restores the open-water frame exactly.

### Batch 67 job 2: wave subsurface scattering
water/scatter makes backlit wave crests glow with a tint, in shadow-map visibility, without changing geometry. Evidence: packages/water/test/scatter.test.js (atomic validation, copies, dispose) and scatter.browser.mjs (WGSL term matches an independent JavaScript evaluation of the formula within 1e-5; a light ahead of the camera tints the water, a light behind it adds under a quarter of that, strength 0 matches the plain frame within 1, disposing restores the plain frame exactly). Not claimed: physical light transport or measured thickness.

### Batch 64 job 6: procedural grass blades
scatter/grass draws meadows with no mesh input: the vertex stage builds each blade of an instance's clump from a hashed Bezier spine. Proof: packages/scatter/test/grass.test.js (ring encoding, conservative cull box, shape validation, the pose wrapper) and packages/scatter/test/grass.browser.mjs on real WebGPU (diffuse and PBR shaders compile and draw; 16 blades a clump cover over 3 times the pixels of 1 on identical instance rings; doubled height covers more). Picture stand-in: procedural meadow, no reference image from another tool. No speed claim.

### List 3 batch 66 job 8: steep-bed rivers (2026-11-10)

Optional water/rivers runs Water's terrain-coupled shallow water over waterfalls and steep chutes with hydrostatically reconstructed pipes, a Froude and Courant velocity cap and up to 16 inflows. Proof: packages/water/test/rivers.browser.mjs on the M6 Mac mini (Metal WebGPU and the compatibility layer) matches an independent float64 model on a 10 m fall and chute within 9.2e-6 m after 1920 substeps (bound 1e-3), water reaches the chute (0.173 m on both), and dispose hands the coast back dry. A lake at rest on a stepped bed stays exactly at rest (packages/water/test/rivers.test.js). Measured cost on a 256 x 256 map, 8 substeps, M6 Mac mini Metal WebGPU while other lanes ran: 0.168 ms per frame against 0.155 ms for Water's own 3-substep coast (diagnostic, not a release timing).

### Batch 67 job 3: rain ripples
water/rain puts rain rings on Water that follow a weather's rain, and can drop splashes into a Ripples field. Evidence: packages/water/test/rain.test.js (validation, weather following and clamping, time, dispose) and rain.browser.mjs (WGSL ring slope matches an independent JavaScript evaluation with the same PCG hash within 2e-4 over 578 points; rain 0 matches the plain frame within 1; WaterFlow and WaterRain keep both normals in either attach order; splashes reach the field at the configured rate and place; disposing restores the plain frame exactly). Not claimed: drop physics or measured rain spectra.

### Batch 203 job 4: owned AVIF still-image decoding

The optional decoders/avif entry uses one clean-room Rust SIMD asset in browser and Node workers. Its 16-file corpus matches 14,828,077 independent 8/10/12-bit YUV samples exactly, including palette, lossless/lossy/subsampled intra copy, film grain, Wiener/SGR, qmatrices/four tiles and super-resolution. ## Batch 120: independent domain installs

Batch 120 adds standalone @margoengine/game, /web, /viz and /react-kit install profiles. Root forwarding entries measure 740, 321, 578 and 158 B min+gzip respectively, previously absent (0 B); their strict quality ceilings are 996, 577, 834 and 485 B. Member alias entries reuse the original package implementation and bytes. The core + stage root remains 24,209 B min+gzip before and after. Model loading is installed but exposed through /gltf-pro, so a root-only app emits no decoder files. Packed native installs contain 70/40/42/42 engine packages versus 98 for the umbrella; React additionally installs its explicit React and React DOM peers. Exact dated installed/packed byte snapshots and closure definitions are in docs/guides/kit-install-sizes.json and docs/guides/kits.md; these are file-byte measurements, not speed claims. Optional third-party peers and the optional WebGL2 backend are not part of a plain profile install.

Proof: scripts/kit-install-check.mjs installs only packed manifests through a read-only loopback registry, compares required closures, rejects foreign engine downloads and mixed versions, and builds real applications with engine resolution isolated inside their node_modules. tests/kit-boundaries.test.js and each profile test check generated aliases and core/stage boundaries. The separate shared-GPU check inspects nonuniform rendered pixels and rejects page errors on native WebGPU and full WebGL2. No timing advantage is claimed.

### Batch 176 AMR: block ownership and direct rendering
Claim: sciviz/amr selects the finest valid Cartesian block, masks covered coarse cells, slices original cells and extracts contours across a shared lattice. Direct volume rays traverse a disjoint k-d partition rather than a dense finest-level resample. Proof: packages/sciviz/tests/amr.test.js checks exact Nyx bytes/masks against yt, analytic ray/slice conservation and cross-level contours. packages/sciviz/tests/amr-browser.mjs renders the licensed real AMReX timestep on WebGPU and WebGL2 compatibility, including density/MIP/clipping/restoration. This is a correctness claim; interpolation, format and sample limits are in limits.md, and no performance parity claim is made.




## Batch 175 job 3: exact table raster bins
The optional aggregate entry rasterises every primitive row for points, segments, areas, triangles and quads on WebGPU or the WebGL2 compatibility GPU, computes count/sum/mean/min/max/first/last and category counts, and has an exact CPU reference. Binary64 superaccumulators preserve cancellation and chunk/order independence; shading and spreading leave original hover statistics unchanged. Proof: packages/sciviz/tests/aggregate.test.js and aggregate-browser.mjs; retained results: results/batch175-aggregate-webgpu.json and results/batch175-aggregate-webgl2.json. WebGL2 records 15 GPU dispatches and zero CPU compute dispatches for five glyphs. WebGPU processes 100000000 physical rows, repeating 4096 original TLC observations; every count and numeric reduction matches an independent original-coordinate/integer-cent oracle. No claim of 100M distinct trips, wall-clock performance, vertex interpolation or fractional coverage. Contract and research: ../sciviz-aggregate.md.
- Batch 174 job 6: sciviz/views links an arbitrary number of same-dimensionality figure cameras or selected physical axes, creates comparative grids from one PipelineJSON across time/parameters, and builds a T1 quad with three orthographic slices, matching 3D planes and a shared nearest-sample physical crosshair. Physical slices retain isotropic display on resize. Proof: packages/sciviz/tests/views.test.js and views-browser.mjs; both backends, actual linked pointer drag, real OpenNeuro T1 and NOAA daily wind data, independent graph outputs and lifecycle checks. Several figures are used, each owning its resources. No oblique-reslicing, shared-memory, medical-accuracy or performance claim. Research and behavioural coverage: docs/clean-room.md, batch 174 job 6.

### Sciviz review L2: bounded density spreading

aggregate.test.js proves that an isolated mark stops at the five-pixel radius-1 footprint for spread 4 and density 0.5, instead of the old 49-pixel disc. Dense and empty images, clipped edges, the one-pixel cap and unchanged original counts are covered. Native/compatibility shading integration remains in aggregate-browser.mjs. The density is the fraction of occupied pixels with an occupied eight-neighbour; see ../sciviz-aggregate.md for sources and the full contract. No speed claim.

## Batch 180 job 5: optional temporal analysis

@margoengine/sciviz/temporal provides per-element mean/minimum/maximum/population or sample standard deviation, irregular-step linear/nearest/step interpolation, physical-position probe histories with native gap-preserving charts, numbered and .series JSON file grouping, and explicit fixed time-unit/origin conversion. Proof: packages/sciviz/tests/temporal.test.js, with independent two-pass reductions and exact known values; packages/sciviz/tests/temporal-browser.mjs renders real NOAA days and ERA5 six-hour samples on both backends and proves actual restoration, cancellation rollback and disposal. The numerical feature is independent of the rendering backend. There is no claim of general calendar decoding, duration-weighted averaging or benchmark superiority. The optional entry is 3,901 B min+gzip, previously absent, with a 4,157 B ceiling. Core + stage remains 24,209 B. Data provenance is examples/assets/sciviz/temporal/source.json; API behaviour and limits are docs/sciviz-temporal.md.

### Batch 181 density scatter

The optional sciviz/density entry preserves all in-frustum/scissor point votes as u32 counts, independently of pointBudget, and provides count opacity plus a four-sigma separable Gaussian over the screen histogram. Independent analytical projection/opacity/Gaussian tests, exact table/filter tests and real device replacement run on both backends. Every raster pixel agrees with repeated real observations at 20M 2MASS and 10M NYC TLC; a coincident count of 16,777,217 remains exact. Source upload buffers are chunked to device limits. Compatibility atomic accumulation uses its CPU compute tier, so this is correctness and feature evidence, not a GPU-throughput claim. Limits and accepted visual differences are in ../sciviz-density.md. Timed rival comparison remains speed-phase owed.

Density pixel comparison evidence is frozen in results/batch181-density.json: actual Margo image versus regl-scatterplot 1.16.0 at 20M 2MASS and deck.gl 9.4.1 at 10M TLC, 256 x 256. Mean red-byte differences are 0.1480712891 and 0.0787353516, maxima 246 and 255, with 0.2227783203% and 0.1724243164% of pixels above two bytes. Margo's image has zero mean difference from independent count opacity. Non-parity and policy/rounding attribution limits are retained in ../sciviz-density.md; this is no throughput claim.

# Batch 175 job 6: scientific reader workers

The optional sciviz/workers entry executes NRRD, NIfTI, DICOM, Zarr, legacy VTK, NetCDF and VTK XML readers off the page through worker/rpc.
